∎

To conclude, let us study head-reduction
\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}h}
in
\Lambda^{\{\}}_{\mathsf{{PE}}}
.

Randomized contexts
\mathtt{R}[\ ]
for
\Lambda^{\{\}}_{\mathsf{{PE}}}
are defined as for
\Lambda_{\mathsf{{PE}}}
.

Head-contexts

\mathtt{H}[\ ]
for
\Lambda_{\mathsf{{PE}}}
are defined by the grammar below:

\mathtt{H}[\ ]:=[\ ]\mid\lambda x.\mathtt{H}[\ ]\mid\mathtt{H}[\ ]u\mid\{\mathtt{H}[\ ]\}u

Head-reductions
t\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}h}u
are defined inductively as being either a
\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}}
-reduction or a
\beta
-reduction of one of the following two forms

\displaystyle\mathtt{R}[\mathtt{H}[(\lambda y.t)u]]

\displaystyle\rightarrowtriangle_{\beta}\mathtt{R}[\mathtt{H}[t[u/y]]]

\displaystyle\mathtt{R}[\mathtt{H}[\{t\}u]]

\displaystyle\rightarrowtriangle\mathtt{R}[\mathtt{H}[\{t\}u^{\prime}]]

where
u\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}h}u^{\prime}
.

Let
{T}^{\{\}}
be the set of PNF with respect to
\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}\{\}}
.
A pseudo-value
t
is a PNF which is either a
\lambda
, a variable, an application
tu
or a CbV application
\{t\}u
.
We let
{V}^{\{\}}
indicate the set of pseudo-values.

The definition of
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)
is extended from
\Lambda_{\mathsf{{PE}}}
to
\Lambda^{\{\}}_{\mathsf{{PE}}}
simply by adding the condition

\pi^{\omega}_{X}(\{t\}u)=\{\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\}\pi^{\omega}_{X}(u)
.
In this way, a sub-distribution
{D}_{t}:{V}^{\{\}}\to[0,1]
can be defined as in Section
3
.

Lemma B.12.

A pseudo-value
t\in{V}^{\{\}}
is in head normal form iff either
t=\mathtt{H}[x]
or
t=\mathtt{H}[\{t\}u]
, where

u\in{V}^{\{\}}
and is
\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}}
-normal.

A head normal value is defined as in
\Lambda_{\mathsf{{PE}}}
as a pseudo-value in head normal form. We let HNV
{}
indicate the set of head normal values.
Using Lemma
B.12
we can define, in analogy with the case of
\Lambda_{\mathsf{{PE}}}
, the functions
\mathrm{HNV}(t):=\sum_{v\in\mathrm{HNV}^{\{\}}}{D}_{t}(v)
, and

\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}(t):=\sup\{\mathrm{HNV}(u)\mid t\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}h}^{*}u\}
.

Appendix C
Details about
\mathbf{C}\lambda^{\{\}}_{\to}
.

C.1.
Subject Reduction.

The goal of this section is to establish the following result:

Proposition C.1 (Subject Reduction).

If
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
and
t\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}}u
, then

\Gamma\vdash^{X}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
.

With the goal of making proof slightly simpler, in the formulation of
\mathbf{C}\lambda^{\{\}}_{\to}
we replace the rule (
\oplus
) with the two rules (
\oplus l
) and (
\oplus r
) below:

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}

(
\oplus l
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t\oplus_{a}^{i}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\land\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}

(
\oplus r
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t\oplus_{a}^{i}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

It is easily checked that, in presence of the rule (
\vee
), having the rule (
\oplus
) is equivalent to having the rules (
\oplus l
) and (
\oplus r
).

To establish the subject reduction property, we first need to establish a few auxiliary lemmas.

Lemma C.2.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
and
X\subseteq Y
, then

\Gamma\vdash^{Y}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
.

Proof.

By induction on a type derivation of
t
.
∎

Lemma C.3.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
holds and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\vDash^{X}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}
, then

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
is derivable by a derivation of the same length.

Proof.

By induction on a type derivation of
t
.
∎

Lemma C.4 (substitution lemma).

The following rule is derivable:

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{t}

\Gamma\vdash^{X}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}

(subst)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t[u/x]:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{t}

Proof.

We argue by induction on the typing derivation of
t
:

\bullet

if the last rule is

\mathsf{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b})\subseteq X

(id)

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X}x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

then
t[u/x]=u
, so the claim can be deduced using Lemma
C.3
.

\bullet

if the last rule is

\left\{\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{t}\right\}_{i}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\vDash^{X}\bigvee_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}

(\lor)

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X}x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{t}

Then, by IH, we deduce
\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X}t[u/x]:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{t}
, and since

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}\bigvee_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d})
we conclude by applying an instance of (
\lor
).

\bullet

if the last rule is

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t_{1}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{t}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}^{\prime}

(
\oplus l
)

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t_{1}\oplus^{i}_{a}t_{2}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{t}

Then, by IH, we deduce

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},q}t_{1}[u/x]:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}
.
From
t[u/x]=(t_{1}[u/x])\oplus_{a}^{i}(t_{2}[u/x])
and the fact that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d})
, we deduce the claim by an instance of the same rule.

\bullet

if the last rule is

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t_{2}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{t}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}^{\prime}

(\oplus r)

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t_{1}\oplus^{i}_{a}t_{2}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{t}

we can argue similarly to the previous case.

\bullet

if the last rule is

\Gamma,y:\mathfrak{t}^{\prime},x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X}t^{\prime}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}\tau

({\lambda})

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X}\lambda y.t^{\prime}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}(\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}\Rightarrow\tau)

Then, by IH, we deduce
\Gamma,y:\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}\vdash^{X}t^{\prime}[u/x]:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}\tau
and since
t[u/x]=(\lambda y.t^{\prime})[u/x]=\lambda y.t^{\prime}[u/x]
we conclude by applying an instance of the same rule.

\bullet

if the last rule is

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X}t_{1}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{1}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}(\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}\Rightarrow\tau)

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X}t_{2}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{2}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\vDash^{X}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{1}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{2}

(
@
)

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X}t_{1}t_{2}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}\tau

Then, by IH, we deduce
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t_{1}[u/x]:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{1}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}(\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}\Rightarrow\tau)
and

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t_{2}[u/x]:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{2}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}
, and since
(t_{1}t_{2})[u/x]=(t_{1}[u/x])(t_{2}[u/x])
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{1}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d})\land({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{2}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d})
,
we conclude by applying an instance of the same rule.

\bullet

if the last rule is

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X}t_{1}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{1}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}(\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}\Rightarrow\tau)

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X}t_{2}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{2}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{s}\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\vDash^{X}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{1}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{2}

(
\{\}
)

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X}\{t_{1}\}t_{2}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{s}\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}\tau

Then, by IH, we deduce
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t_{1}[u/x]:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{1}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}(\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}\Rightarrow\tau)
and

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t_{2}[u/x]:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{2}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{s}\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}
, and since
\{t_{1}\}t_{2})[u/x]=\{t_{1}[u/x]\}(t_{2}[u/x])
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{1}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d})\land({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{2}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d})
,
we conclude by applying an instance of the same rule.

\bullet

if the last rule is

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{t}

\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e})\geq s

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\vDash^{X}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}^{\prime}

(\mu)

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X}\nu a.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{s}\mathfrak{t}

Then, by IH,

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t[u/x]:({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d})\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{t}
.
Hence, from the fact that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}
and that
a
cannot occur in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}
, and since

(\nu a.t)[u/x]=\nu a.t[u/x]
, we can deduce the claim by applying an instance of the same rule.

∎

We now have all ingredients to establish the subject reduction property of
\mathbf{C}\lambda^{\{\}}_{\to}
.

Proof of Proposition
C.1
.

First observe that if the typing derivation
D
of
t
ends by a
(\lor)
-rule, it suffices to establish the property for the immediate sub-derivations of
D
and then apply an instance of
(\lor)
-rule to the resulting derivations. So we will always suppose that the typing derivation of
D
does not end by a
(\lor)
-rule.

For the case of
\beta
-reduction it suffices to check the claim when
t
is a redex
(\lambda x.t_{1})t_{2}
and
u
is
t_{1}[t_{2}/x]
. From

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
we can suppose w.l.o.g. that
the typing derivation is as below:

\left\{\hbox{\ignorespaces\minipage{345.0pt}$\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 16.38667pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 11.80556pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{t}\vdash^{X}t_{1}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}\sigma$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to121.87927pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\vdash^{X}\lambda x.t_{1}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}(\mathfrak{t}\Rightarrow\sigma)$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\ignorespaces$
\endminipage
}\right\}_{i}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{1}\vDash\bigvee_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}

(\lor)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}\lambda x.t_{1}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{1}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}(\mathfrak{t}\Rightarrow\sigma)

\left\{\Gamma\vdash^{X}t_{2}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{j}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{t}\right\}_{j}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{2}\vDash\bigvee_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{j}

(\lor)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t_{2}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{2}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{t}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{1}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{2}

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}\sigma

From Lemma
C.4
we deduce the existence of derivations of

\Gamma\vdash^{X}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{j}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}\sigma
;
from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{1}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{2}
we deduce then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}\bigvee_{i,j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{j}
, using the fact that

\bigvee_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\land\bigvee_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{j}\equiv\bigvee_{i,j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{j}
. We can thus conclude as follows:

\left\{\Gamma\vdash^{X}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{j}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}\sigma\right\}_{i,j}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}\bigvee_{i,j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{j}

(\lor)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}\sigma

For the case of
\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}\{\}}
wee consider reduction rules one by one.

(
t\oplus_{a}^{i}t\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}\{\}}t
):

The last rule of
t
is either

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}

(
\oplus l
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t\oplus^{a}_{i}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

or

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}

(
\oplus r
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t\oplus^{a}_{i}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

Then, in either case, from
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}
, using Lemma
C.3
we deduce
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
.

(
(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)\oplus_{a}^{i}v\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}\{\}}t\oplus_{a}^{i}v
):

There are three possible sub-cases:

(1):

the type derivation is as follows:

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\vDash^{X}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}

({\oplus}l)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t\oplus_{a}^{i}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}

({\oplus}l)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)\oplus_{a}^{i}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

Then from
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
and since we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vdash^{X}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}
we deduce
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t\oplus_{a}^{i}v:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
.

(2):

the type derivation is as follows:

\Gamma\vdash^{X}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\vDash^{X}\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}

(\oplus r)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t\oplus_{a}^{i}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}

({\oplus}l)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)\oplus_{a}^{i}v:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

Then, from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\vDash^{X}\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}
we deduce
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bot}
, so we conclude
\Gamma\vdash^{X}(t\oplus_{a}^{i}v):{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
using one of the initial rules.

(3):

the type derivation is as follows:

\Gamma\vdash^{X}v:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}

(\oplus r)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)\oplus_{a}^{i}v:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

Then from
\Gamma\vdash^{X}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}
we deduce
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t\oplus_{a}^{i}v:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
.

(
t\oplus_{a}^{i}(u\oplus_{a}^{i}v)\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}\{\}}t\oplus_{a}^{i}v
):

Similar to the case above.

(
\lambda x.(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}\{\}}(\lambda x.t)\oplus_{a}^{i}(\lambda x.u)
):

There are two possible sub-cases:

1.:

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}\tau

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}

({\oplus}l)

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X}t\oplus_{a}^{i}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}\tau

({\lambda})

\Gamma\vdash^{X}\lambda x.(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u):{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}(\mathfrak{s}\Rightarrow\tau)

Then, we deduce

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}\tau

({\lambda})

\Gamma\vdash^{X}\lambda x.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}(\mathfrak{s}\Rightarrow\tau)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}

({\oplus}l)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}(\lambda x.t)\oplus_{a}^{i}(\lambda x.u):{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}(\mathfrak{s}\Rightarrow\tau)

2.:

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}\tau

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}

(\oplus r)

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X}t\oplus_{a}^{i}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}\tau

({\lambda})

\Gamma\vdash^{X}\lambda x.(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u):{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}(\mathfrak{s}\Rightarrow\tau)

Then, we can argue similarly to the previous case.

(
(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)v\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}\{\}}(tv)\oplus_{a}^{i}(uv)
):

There are two possible sub-cases:

(1):

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}(\mathfrak{s}\Rightarrow\tau)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\vDash^{X}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}

({\oplus}l)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t\oplus_{a}^{i}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}(\mathfrak{s}\Rightarrow\tau)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}v:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}

({@})

\Gamma\vdash^{X}(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)v:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}\tau

Then, we deduce

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}(\mathfrak{s}\Rightarrow\tau)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}v:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\vDash^{X}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}

({@})

\Gamma\vdash^{X}tv:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}\tau

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}

({\oplus}l)

Appendix D
Details about
\mathbf{C}\lambda_{\to,\cap}
.

D.1.
Subject Reduction.

In this subsection we show that subject reduction holds for
\mathbf{C}\lambda_{\to,\cap}
(and a fortiori for
\mathbf{C}\lambda_{\to}
).

Proposition D.1 (Subject Reduction).

If
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
and
t\rightarrowtriangle u
, then

\Gamma\vdash^{X}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
.

As for
\mathbf{C}\lambda^{\{\}}_{\to}
(see Section
C
), we replace the rule (
\oplus
) by the two rules (
\oplus l
) and (
\oplus r
).
Moreover, we will ignore the rules (
\mathsf{hn}
) and (
\mathsf{n}
), as the result extends immediately to them.

To show the reduction property of
\mathbf{C}\lambda_{\to,\cap}
we need to establish a few lemmas, some of which are analogous to results for
\mathbf{C}\lambda^{\{\}}_{\to}
, and proved in a similar way:

Lemma D.2.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
and
X\subseteq Y
, then

\Gamma\vdash^{Y}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
.

Lemma D.3.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
holds and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\vDash^{X}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}
, then

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
is derivable by a derivation of the same length.

The next lemmas are new:

Lemma D.4.

The following rule is admissible in
\mathbf{C}\lambda_{\to,\cap}
:

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

\mathfrak{s}\preceq\mathfrak{t}

(\preceq)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{t}

Proof.

We will show the admissibility of a more general rule, namely

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{t}

\Delta\preceq\Gamma,\mathfrak{s}\preceq\mathfrak{t}

(\preceq^{*})

\Delta\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{t}

where
\Delta\preceq\Gamma
holds when
\Gamma=\{x_{1}:\mathfrak{M}_{1},\dots,x_{n}:\mathfrak{M}_{n}\}
,

\Delta=\{x_{1}:\mathfrak{N}_{1},\dots,x_{n}:\mathfrak{N}_{n}\}
and
\mathfrak{M}_{i}\preceq\mathfrak{N}_{i}
.

We argue by induction on a typing derivation
D
of
t
:

\bullet

if
D
is

\mathrm{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b})\subseteq X

\mathfrak{s}_{i}\preceq\mathfrak{t}

\Gamma,x:[\mathfrak{s}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{s}_{n}]\vdash^{X}x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{t}

then from
\Delta\preceq\Gamma,x:[\mathfrak{s}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{s}_{n}]
we deduce that
\Delta
contains
x:\mathfrak{M}
, with
\mathfrak{M}\preceq[\mathfrak{s}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{s}_{n}]
. This implies that
\mathfrak{M}
contains
\mathfrak{u}_{i}
, where
\mathfrak{u}_{i}\preceq\mathfrak{s}_{i}
; by transitivity of
\preceq
, from
\mathfrak{u}_{i}\preceq\mathfrak{s}_{i}\preceq\mathfrak{t}
, we deduce
\mathfrak{u}_{i}\preceq\mathfrak{t}
, and thus we can construct the derivation below

\mathrm{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b})\subseteq X

\mathfrak{u}_{i}\preceq\mathfrak{t}

\Delta\vdash^{X}x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{t}

\bullet

if
D
ends by any of the rules
(\lor)
,
(\oplus l)
,
(\oplus r)
or
(\mu_{\Sigma})
, then we can directly conclude by applying the I.H.

\bullet

if
D
is

\vdots

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{M}\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma

(\lambda)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}\lambda x.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma)

then from
\mathbf{C}^{q}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma)\preceq\mathfrak{t}
we deduce
\mathfrak{t}=\mathbf{C}^{s}(\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}\Rightarrow\sigma^{\prime})
, with
s\leq q
,

\mathfrak{M}\preceq\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
and
\sigma\preceq\sigma^{\prime\prime}
, and from
\Delta\preceq\Gamma
, we deduce
\Delta,x:\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}\preceq\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{M}
. So by the I.H. we deduce the existence of a derivation of
\Delta,x:\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma^{\prime}
and we can conclude by applying an instance of
(\lambda)
.

\bullet

if
D
is

\vdots

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma)

\left\{\hbox{\ignorespaces\minipage{345.0pt}$\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 5.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 30.47002pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdots$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}\hbox{\vbox{\vskip 1.0pt}}\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\vdash^{X}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}_{i}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\ignorespaces$
\endminipage
}\right\}_{i}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\land\bigwedge_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i}

@_{\cap}

\Gamma\vdash^{X}tu:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma

where
\mathfrak{M}=[\mathfrak{s}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{s}_{n}]
.
Then from
\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma\preceq\mathfrak{s}
we deduce
\tau=\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma^{\prime}
, with
s\leq q
and
\sigma\preceq\sigma^{\prime}
, and thus
\mathbf{C}^{q}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma)\preceq\mathbf{C}^{s}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma^{\prime})
,
so by the I.H. applied to the left-hand side sub-derivation we obtain a the existence of a derivation of
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{s}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma^{\prime})
, and thus we can conclude by applying an instance of
(@_{\cap})
.

∎

Lemma D.5.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{1},\dots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{n}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{1},\dots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{n}
be such that
\mathsf{FN}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}}),\mathsf{FN}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}})\subseteq{X}
and
\mathsf{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}),\mathsf{FN}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}})\subseteq\{a\}
, where
a\notin X
.
If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}
is satisfiable, then if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}\bigvee_{i}^{n}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}
holds,
also
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}\bigvee_{i}^{n}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}
holds.

Proof.

Let
v\in 2^{X}
be a model of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}
. Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}
is satisfiable,
v
can be extended to a model
v^{\prime}\in 2^{X\cup\{a\}}
of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}
. By hypothesis, then
v^{\prime}
satisfies
\bigvee_{i}^{n}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}
, so for some
i_{0}\leq n
, it satisfies
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i_{0}}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i_{0}}
. We deduce then that
v
satisfies
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i_{0}}
, and thus
v
satisfies
\bigvee_{i}^{n}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}
.
∎

Lemma D.6.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup Y}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
is derivable, where
X\cap Y=\emptyset
,
\mathrm{FN}(t)\subseteq X
,

\mathrm{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b})\subseteq X
,
\mathrm{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c})\subseteq Y
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}
is satisfiable, then
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
is also derivable.

Proof.

By induction on a typing derivation of
t
:

\bullet

if the last rule is

\mathfrak{s}_{i}\preceq\mathfrak{t}

\mathsf{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c})\subseteq X\cup Y

(id
∩
)

\Gamma,x:[\mathfrak{s}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{s}_{n}]\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{t}

then the claim can be deduced by an instance of the same rule.

\bullet

if the last rule is

\left\{\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup Y}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}\right\}_{i}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\vDash^{X\cup Y}\bigvee_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}

(\lor)

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup Y}x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

Let
\bigvee_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{ij}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ij}
be weak
Y
-decompositions of the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}
(see [
[
AntonelliArXiv
]
, p. 17]);
since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{ij}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ij}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}
, by Lemma
D.3
we deduce
that
\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup Y}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{ij}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ij}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
is derivable for all
i
and
j
, by a derivation of same length as the corresponding derivation of
\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup Y}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
;
hence we can apply the I.H. to such derivations, yielding derivations of

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{ij}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
.

Using Lemma
D.5
, from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\vDash\bigvee_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}

, so we conclude

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}\bigvee_{ij}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{ij}

(\lor)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

\bullet

if the last rule is

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{i}^{b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}

({\oplus}l)

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup Y}t_{1}\oplus^{i}_{b}t_{2}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

Then, let

-decomposition of

by Lemma

there exist derivations of

Lemma D.7 (substitution lemma).

The following rule is derivable:

\Gamma,x:[\mathfrak{s}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{s}_{n}]\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\tau

\{\Gamma\vdash^{X}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}_{i}\}_{i=1,\dots,n}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\land\left(\bigwedge_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i}\right)

(subst
∩
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t[u/x]:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\tau

We now have all elements to establish the subject reduction property.

Proof of Proposition
D.1
.

As in the proof of Prop.
C.1
, observe that if the typing derivation
D
of
t
ends by a
(\lor)
-rule, it suffices to establish the property for the immediate sub-derivations of
D
and then apply an instance of
(\lor)
-rule to the resulting derivations. So we will always suppose that the typing derivation of
D
does not end by a
(\lor)
-rule.

The argument for
\beta
-reduction works similarly to the one in the Prop.
C.1
, using Lemma
D.7
in place of Lemma
C.4
. (subst
∩
)

For the case of
\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}
wee consider reduction rules one by one. Most cases are analogous to those from the proof of Prop.
C.1
. We limit ourselves to the case of the permutation rule (

¬
ν

) (not considered before), and to
those permutations that involve the rule (
\mu_{\Sigma}
):

(
\nu b.(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}(\nu b.t)\oplus_{a}^{i}(\nu b.u)
):

We suppose
a\neq b
. As in the proof of Prop.
C.1
there are two sub-cases, we only consider the first one (the second one being treated similarly):

\left\{\hbox{\ignorespaces\minipage{345.0pt}$\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 14.37985pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a,b\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{i}}\sigma$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to182.4032pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt($\oplus l$)}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 21.1453pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a,b\}}t\oplus_{a}^{i}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{i}}\sigma$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces$
\endminipage
}\right\}_{i}

\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i})\geq s_{i}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}

(
\mu_{\Sigma}
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}\nu b.t\oplus_{a}^{i}u\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}}\sigma

From
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i}
, by Lemma
C.3
, we deduce the existence of a derivation of
\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a,b\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{i}}\sigma
. Moreover, from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i}
and the fact that
\mathrm{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i})\subseteq\{b\}
it follows that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}
, so we can construct the following derivation:

\left\{\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a,b\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{i}}\sigma\right\}_{i}

\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i})\geq s_{i}

(
\mu_{\Sigma}
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}\nu b.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}}\sigma

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}

(
\oplus l
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}(\nu b.t)\oplus_{a}^{i}(\nu b.u):{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}}\sigma

(
\nu a.t\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}t
):

We have

\left\{\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{i}}\sigma\right\}_{i}

\mu(\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\rrbracket_{\{a\}})\geq s_{i}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}

(
\mu_{\Sigma}
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}\nu a.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}}\sigma

Since the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}
are pairwise disjoint, from
\mu({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}})\geq s_{i}
it follows that
\sum_{i}s_{i}\leq 1
. Hence, by letting
Q=\max_{i}\{q_{i}\}
, we have

\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}\leq\sum_{i}Qs_{i}=Q\cdot\sum_{i}s_{i}\leq Q
. Let
i_{0}
be the index such that
Q=q_{i_{0}}
.
Since
a\notin\mathrm{FN}(t)
, by applying Lemma
D.6

to the derivation of
\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i_{0}}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{Q}\sigma
,
we deduce
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{Q}\sigma
, and
since
\mathbf{C}^{Q}\sigma\preceq\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}}\sigma
and, we deduce, by Lemma
D.4
,

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}}\sigma
, and by Lemma
D.3
,

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}}\sigma
.

(
\lambda x.\nu a.t\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}\nu a.\lambda x.t
):

We have

\left\{\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{M}\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{i}}\sigma\right\}_{i}

\mu({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}})\geq s_{i}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}

(
\mu_{\Sigma}
)

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{M}\vdash^{X}\nu a.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}}\sigma

({\lambda})

\Gamma\vdash^{X}\lambda x.\nu a.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma)

from which we deduce:

\left\{\hbox{\ignorespaces\minipage{345.0pt}$\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 13.02151pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\thinspace\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{M}\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{i}}\sigma$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to139.75853pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt$({\lambda})$}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\vdash^{X}\lambda x.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{i}}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma)$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\ignorespaces$
\endminipage
}\right\}_{i}

\mu({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}})\geq s_{i}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}

(
\mu_{\Sigma}
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}\nu a.\lambda x.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma)

(
(\nu a.t)u\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}\nu a.(tu)
):

We have

\left\{\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{i}}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\tau)\right\}_{i}

\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i})\geq s_{i}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}^{\prime}

(
\mu_{\Sigma}
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}\nu a.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\tau)

\Big\{\Gamma\vdash^{X}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{j}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}_{i}\Big\}_{j}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\land\left(\bigwedge_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{j}\right)

(
@_{\cap}
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}(\nu a.t)u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}}\tau

where
\mathfrak{M}=[\mathfrak{s}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{s}_{n}]
,
from which we deduce

\left\{\hbox{\ignorespaces\minipage{345.0pt}$\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 15.27252pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{i}}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\tau)$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Big\{\Gamma\vdash^{X}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{j}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}_{i}\Big\}_{j}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i}\vDash({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i})\land\left(\bigwedge_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{j}\right)$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to360.03601pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt($@_{\cap}$)}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 131.7695pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\vdash^{X}tu:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{i}}\tau$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces$
\endminipage
}\right\}_{i}

\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i})\geq s_{i}

(
\mu_{\Sigma}
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}\nu a.tu:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}}\tau

∎

D.2.
Subject Expansion.

The goal of this section is to establish the following result for
\mathbf{C}\lambda_{\to,\cap}
:

Proposition D.8 (subject expansion).

If
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
and
u\rightarrowtriangle t
, then
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
.

As for subject reduction, we will ignore the rules (
\mathsf{hn}
) and (
\mathsf{n}
), as the result extends immediately to them.

We will consider first the (more laborious case) of
\beta
-reduction, and then the more direct case of permutative reduction.

D.2.1.
Subject
\beta
-Expansion

To establish the subject expansion property we need to develop a finer analysis of typing derivations. In particular we will need to prove that derivations can be turned into a canonical form. The main idea is to reduce to a case where
(\lor)
-rules are always applied “as late as possible”, that is, either at the end of the derivation or right before a counting rule.
Moreover, we will require that if a
(\lor)
-rule occurs before a counting rule for some name
a
, then these two rule follow a specific pattern, defined below, which ensures that no information is loss about other names.

Definition D.9 (
\lor\mu
-pattern).

For any name
a\in\mathbb{N}
and typing derivation
D
, a
\vee\mu
-pattern on
a
in
D
is given by the occurrence in
D
of
(\mu)
-rule preceded by multiple occurrences of
(\vee)
-rules in a configuration as illustrated below:

(1)

\left\{\hbox{\ignorespaces\minipage{345.0pt}$\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 16.63084pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Big\{\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ij}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{i}}\sigma\Big\}_{j=1,\dots,L_{i}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i}\vDash^{X}\bigvee_{l}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ij}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to248.59395pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt$(\lor)$}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 68.37617pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{i}}\sigma$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces$
\endminipage
}\right\}_{i}

\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i})\geq s_{i}

(\mu_{\Sigma})

\Gamma\vdash^{X}\nu a.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}}\sigma

where
\mathrm{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c})\subseteq X
,
\mathrm{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ij})\subseteq\{a\}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}
and the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ij}
are conjunctions of literals.

Definition D.10 (canonical and pseudo-canonical derivation).

A typing derivation
D
is said
canonical
when the following hold:

a.

all Boolean formulas occurring in
D
are conjunctions of literals, except for those which occur either in the conclusion of a
(\lor)
-rule or in the major premiss of a
(\mu_{\Sigma})
-rule;

b.

any occurrence of the
(\lor)
rule in
D
occurs in a
\lor\mu
-pattern.

A typing derivation
D
is said
pseudo-canonical
when
D
is obtained by one application of the
(\lor)
-rule where all premises are conclusions of canonical sub-derivations.

The fundamental property of canonical derivations that we will exploit is the following:

Lemma D.11.

Suppose
D
is a canonical derivation of conclusion
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
.
If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}
is a Boolean formula
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}
with
\mathrm{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c})\subseteq X
occurring in an axiom of
D
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}
.

Proof.

By induction on
D
:

\bullet

if
D
only consists in an axiom, the claim is trivial;

\bullet

if
D
ends by any rule of the form

\{\Delta\vdash??:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{i}\rightarrowtail??\}_{i}

other conditions

\Delta\vdash??:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail??

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{i}
(this includes the rules for
\oplus_{a}^{i}
,
\lambda
and application), then from the induction hypothesis
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{i}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}
holds, where
i
is the index of the derivation where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}
occurs, and we can thus conclude
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}
.

\bullet

if
D
ends by a
\lor\mu
-pattern as in Eq. (
1
), then
by the induction hypothesis

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ij}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}
, where
i
and
j
are chosen so that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}
occurs in the associated sub-derivation. Since
\mathrm{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ij})\cap\mathrm{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c})=\emptyset
and the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ij}
are all satisfiable, we can conclude
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}
by Lemma
D.5
.

∎

Remark 3.

It is not difficult to see that the property of Lemma
D.11
fails for non-canonical derivations. For example, consider the non-canonical derivation below:

x:\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma\vdash^{\{a,b\}}x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{1}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma

(\oplus l)

x:\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma\vdash^{\{a,b\}}x\oplus_{b}^{0}x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{b}^{0}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{1}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma

\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{b}^{0})\geq\frac{1}{2}

(\mu)

x:\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma\vdash^{\{a,b\}}\nu b.x\oplus_{b}^{0}x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{1}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\frac{q}{2}}\sigma

x:\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma\vdash^{\{a,b\}}x:\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{1}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma

(\oplus r)

x:\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma\vdash^{\{a,b\}}x\oplus_{b}^{0}x:\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{b}^{0}\land\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{1}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma

\mu(\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{b}^{0})\geq\frac{1}{2}

(\mu)

x:\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma\vdash^{\{a,b\}}\nu b.x\oplus_{b}^{0}x:\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{1}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\frac{q}{2}}\sigma

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{1}\land\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{1}

(\lor)

x:\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma\vdash^{\{a\}}\nu b.x\oplus_{b}^{0}x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\frac{q}{2}}\sigma

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}=({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{b}^{0}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{1})\vee(\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{b}^{0}\land\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{1})
.
While the literal
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{1}
occurs in an axiom, it is not true that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{1}
.

Observe that the derivation above is pseudo-canonical. Instead, the derivation below is neither canonical nor pseudo-canonical, and similarly violates the property of Lemma
D.11
.

x:\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma\vdash^{\{a,b\}}x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{1}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma

(\oplus l)

x:\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma\vdash^{\{a,b\}}x\oplus_{b}^{0}x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{b}^{0}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{1}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma

x:\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma\vdash^{\{a,b\}}x:\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{1}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma

(\oplus l)

x:\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma\vdash^{\{a,b\}}x\oplus_{b}^{0}x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{b}^{0}\land\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{1}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{b}^{0}\vDash({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{1}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{b}^{0})\lor(\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{1}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{b}^{0})

(\lor)

x:\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma\vdash^{\{a,b\}}x\oplus_{b}^{0}x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{b}^{0}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma

\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{b}^{0})\geq\frac{1}{2}

(\mu)

x:\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma\vdash^{\{a\}}\nu b.x\oplus_{b}^{0}x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\frac{q}{2}}\sigma

The following is the fundamental structural result we need to establish subject
\beta
-expansion.

Theorem D.12.

Any typing derivation can be transformed into a pseudo-canonical derivation.

We need two preliminary lemmas.

Lemma D.13.

Suppose
D
is pseudo-canonical and ends as follows:

\Big\{\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}\Big\}_{i=1,\dots,n}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}\bigvee_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{i}

(\lor)

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

where
\mathrm{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i})\subseteq X
and
\mathrm{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{i})\subseteq\{a\}
.
Then there exists literals
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{ij}
with free names in
X
such that

(i.)

if
i\neq i^{\prime}
, then for all
j,j^{\prime}
either
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{ij}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{ij}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bot}
;

(ii.)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}\equiv\bigvee_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{ij}
, and more generally

\bigvee_{i,j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{ij}\equiv\bigvee_{i=1}^{n}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}
.

Moreover,
D
can be turned into a pseudo-canonical derivation
D^{\prime}
ending as follows:

\Big\{\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{ij}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}\Big\}_{i,j}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}\bigvee_{i,j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{ij}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{i}

(\lor)

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

Proof.

For any of the conjunction of literals
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}
, let
W_{i}\subseteq\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}
contain all pairs
(j,b)
such that
at least one of the literals
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{b}^{j},\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{b}^{j}
occurs in
\bigvee_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}
but
neither of them occurs in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}
. For any such
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}
,
let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i1},\dots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i2^{\sharp W_{i}}}
be the conjunctions of literals obtained by adding to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}
all possible consistent conjunctions of literals chosen from
W_{i}
(i.e. all possible choices between
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{b}^{j}
and
\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{b}^{j}
, for
(j,b)\in W_{i}
). It is clear that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}\equiv\bigvee_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{ij}
and that all
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{ij}
are disjoint, so
ii.
holds.

Moreover, let
i\neq i^{\prime}
and suppose
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{ij}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{j}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}
are not equivalent; since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{ij}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}
are conjunctions of literals, and each of them contains either
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{b}^{j}
or
\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{b}^{j}
for
(j,b)\in\bigcup_{i}W_{i}
, the only possibility is that for some pair
(j,b)
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{ij}
contains either
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{b}^{j}
or
\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{b}^{j}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}
contains its negation, so
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{ij}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bot}
. Hence
i.
also holds.

Finally, the pseudo-canonical derivation
D^{\prime}
is obtained as follows: for any canonical sub-derivation
D_{i}

of
D
of conclusion
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

(whose conclusion contains the Boolean formula
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}
) and for each choice of
j
,
define a sub-derivation
D_{ij}

of conclusion
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{ij}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

by replacing each Boolean formula
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}
occurring in
D_{i}
by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}
, where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{ij}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}
.
Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}
is a conjunction of literals, one can check by induction on
D_{i}
that
D_{ij}
is also canonical.
This is immediate for all rules except for the
(\lor)
-rule. However, since
D_{i}
is canonical,
(\lor)
-rules occur in
D_{i}
only in
\lor\mu
-patterns, and one can check that one obtains then a
\lor\mu
-pattern by replacing each Boolean formula
{a}
by
{a}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}
.

We finally obtain
D^{\prime}
as follows:

\left\{\hbox{\ignorespaces\minipage{345.0pt}$\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 9.91167pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 38.55629pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$D_{ij}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}\hbox{\vbox{\vskip 1.0pt}}\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{ij}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\ignorespaces$
\endminipage
}\right\}_{i,j}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash\bigvee_{i,j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{ij}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{i}

(\lor)

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

∎

Lemma D.14.

If
D
is a derivation ending with a rule whose premises are conclusions of pseudo-canonical derivations, then
D
can be turned into a pseudo-canonical derivation
D^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We consider all possible rules:

\bullet

if
D
is as follows:

(\mu)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}\nu a.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}}\sigma

where

\mathrm{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime})\subseteq X
and
\mathrm{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i})\subseteq\{a\}
, then, since the
D_{ij}
are canonical, each
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ij}
is a conjunction of literals and can thus be written
as
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ij}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}_{ij}
, where
\mathrm{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ij})\subseteq X
and
\mathrm{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}_{ij})\subseteq\{a\}
.

Observe that we can suppose w.l.o.g. that none of either the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ij}
or the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}_{ij}
is equivalent to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bot}
.
Moreover, we can also suppose w.l.o.g. that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}
coincide and are satisfiable.

Now, from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\vDash\bigvee_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ij}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}_{ij}
(using the fact that the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}
are satisfiable) we deduce by Lemma
D.5
that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash\bigvee_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ij}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\vDash\bigvee_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}_{ij}
.

For each
i
, by applying Lemma
D.13
to the family
({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ij})_{j}
,
we obtain a family
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ijk}
of conjunctions of literals, all satisfiable, with
\mathrm{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ijk})\subseteq X
, and such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ij}\equiv\bigvee_{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ijk}
, and for
j\neq j^{\prime}
, either
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ijk}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ij^{\prime}k^{\prime}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ijk}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ij^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bot}
.
Moreover, we also deduce the existence of a family
D_{ijk}^{{\dagger}}
of canonical derivations of conclusion

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},q_{i}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ijk}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}_{ij}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
.

For each
i,j,k
, let
U_{jk}^{i}
be the set of pairs
(j^{\prime},k^{\prime})
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ij^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ijk}
. Observe that for any pair
(j^{\prime\prime},k^{\prime\prime})\notin U_{jk}^{i}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ijk}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ij^{\prime\prime}k^{\prime\prime}}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bot}
.

Let
\approx^{i}
be the equivalence relation on pairs
(j,k)
defined by
(j,k)\approx^{i}(j^{\prime},k^{\prime})
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ijk}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ij^{\prime}k^{\prime}}
(i.e. iff
(j^{\prime},k^{\prime})\in U_{jk}^{i}
), and let
W^{i}
contain a chosen representative of
[(j,k)]_{\approx^{i}}
for each equivalence class.
Then we have that

(2)

\bigvee_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ij}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}_{ij}\equiv\bigvee_{jk}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ijk}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}_{ij}\equiv\bigvee_{(j,k)\in W^{i}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ijk}\land\Big(\bigvee_{j^{\prime}k^{\prime}\in U^{i}_{jk}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}_{ij^{\prime}}\Big)

Let
\widetilde{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}_{ijk}}=\bigvee_{j^{\prime}k^{\prime}\in U^{i}_{jk}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}_{ij^{\prime}}
.
Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\equiv\bigvee_{u}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{u}
, where the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{u}
are pairwise disjoint conjunctions of literals, all being satisfiable.
From
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\vDash\bigvee_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ij}\equiv\bigvee_{(j,k)\in W^{i}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ijk}\land\widetilde{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}_{ijk}}

we deduce
by Lemma
D.5
and the satisfiability of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}
that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash\bigvee_{(j,k)\in W^{i}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ijk}
;
since the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ijk}
, with
(j,k)
varying in

, are pairwise disjoint,
this implies the existence of functions

such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{u}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{iJ(u)K(u)}
, and thus a fortiori such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{u}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{iJ(u)K(u)}\land\widetilde{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}_{iJ(u)K(u)}}
. Since the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{u}
are satisfiable, by Lemma
D.5
, this implies

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\vDash\widetilde{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}_{iJ(u)K(u)}}
.

For each
u
, we can now construct a derivation

of
\Gamma\vdash^{X}\nu a.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{iJ(u)K(u)}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}}\sigma
as shown below:

\left\{\hbox{\ignorespaces\minipage{345.0pt}$\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 22.08804pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\left\{\hbox{\ignorespaces\minipage{345.0pt}$\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 11.16559pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 45.41289pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$D^{{\ddagger}}_{ij^{\prime}k^{\prime}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}\hbox{\vbox{\vskip 1.0pt}}\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{u}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}_{ij^{\prime}}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{i}}\sigma$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\ignorespaces$
\endminipage
}\right\}_{(j^{\prime},k^{\prime})\in U^{i}_{J(u)K(u)}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{u}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\vDash\bigvee_{j^{\prime}k^{\prime}\in U_{J(u)K(u)}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{u}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}_{ij^{\prime}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to581.87267pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt$(\lor)$}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 232.84015pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{u}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{i}}\sigma$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces$
\endminipage
}\right\}_{i}

\mu\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\right)\geq s_{i}

(\mu)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}\nu a.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{u}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}}\sigma

\endminipage

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash\bigvee_{u}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{u}
