\Gamma\vdash^{X}\nu a.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}}\sigma

∎

We can now prove Theorem
D.12
.

Proof of Theorem
D.12
.

First, for any axiom

\mathfrak{s}_{i}\preceq\mathfrak{t}

\mathrm{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b})\subseteq X

(\mathrm{id}_{\preceq})

\Gamma,x:[\mathfrak{s}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{s}_{n}]\vdash^{X}x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{t}

transform
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}
into an equivalent disjunctive normal form
\bigvee_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{j}
(with the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{j}
conjunctions of literals), and replace the axiom by the derivation below

\left\{\hbox{\ignorespaces\minipage{345.0pt}$\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 11.13249pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 12.10594pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathfrak{s}_{i}\preceq\mathfrak{t}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathrm{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b})\subseteq X$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-2.64728pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to122.10599pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.84898pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt$(\mathrm{id}_{\preceq})$}}\vskip-2.64726pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma,x:[\mathfrak{s}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{s}_{n}]\vdash^{X}x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{j}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{t}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\ignorespaces$
\endminipage
}\right\}_{j}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}\bigvee_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{j}

(\lor)

\Gamma,x:[\mathfrak{s}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{s}_{n}]\vdash^{X}x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{t}

Now, starting from the axioms, for each rule occurring in the construction of the derivation, use Lemma
D.14
to progressively permute
(\lor)
-rules downwards, hence turning the derivation into a pseudo-canonical one.
∎

Using Theorem
D.12
we can finally prove the subject
\beta
-expansion property.
We will make use of the following property (which is an easy consequence of Lemma
D.6
and Lemma
D.3
):

Lemma D.15.

If
\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^{X\cup Y}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
is derivable, where
\mathrm{FV}(t)\subseteq\Gamma
and
\mathrm{FN}(t)\subseteq X
, then there exists a Boolean formula
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}
with
\mathrm{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime})\subseteq X
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}
and

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
is derivable.

Proposition D.16 (subject
\beta
-expansion).

If
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t[u/x]:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
is derivable then

\Gamma\vdash^{X}(\lambda x.t)u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
is also derivable.

Proof.

A typing derivation of
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t[u/x]:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
can be depicted as follows:

D_{1}

\Gamma,\Delta_{1}\vdash^{X\cup Y_{1}}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}_{1}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}_{1}

\dots

D_{N}

\Gamma,\Delta_{N}\vdash^{X\cup Y_{N}}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}_{N}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}_{N}

D

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t[u/x]:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

Observe that neither the variables in
\Delta_{i}
nor the names in
Y_{i}
can occur in
u
. Hence using Lemma
D.15
we obtain derivations
D^{*}_{i}
of
\Gamma\vdash^{X}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}_{i}
, with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}_{i}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i}
.

Moreover, from
D
we can deduce a derivation
D^{*}
of
\Gamma,x:[\mathfrak{s}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{s}_{N}]\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
, where each of the
D_{i}
is replaced by an axiom

\mathfrak{s}_{i}\preceq\mathfrak{s}_{i}

\mathrm{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i})\subseteq X

(\mathrm{id}_{\preceq})

\Gamma,\Delta_{i},x:[\mathfrak{s}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{s}_{N}]\vdash^{X}x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}_{i}

and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}
is such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}
.

Using Theorem
D.12
we can turn
D^{*}
into a pseudo-canonical derivation
D^{*{\dagger}}
, which is thus of the form

D^{*{\dagger}}\quad=\quad\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 17.37465pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\left\{\hbox{\ignorespaces\minipage{345.0pt}$\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 8.41666pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 51.0314pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$E_{l}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}\hbox{\vbox{\vskip 1.0pt}}\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma,x:[\mathfrak{s}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{s}_{N}]\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{l}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\ignorespaces$
\endminipage
}\right\}_{l}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\vDash\bigvee_{l}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{l}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to426.64246pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt$(\lor)$}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 152.92584pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma,x:[\mathfrak{s}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{s}_{N}]\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces

where each
E_{l}
is canonical and contains all axioms of the form

\mathfrak{s}_{i}\preceq\mathfrak{s}_{i}

\mathrm{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ig_{l}(i)})\subseteq X

(\mathrm{id}_{\preceq})

\Gamma,\Delta_{i},x:[\mathfrak{s}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{s}_{N}]\vdash^{X}x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ig_{l}(i)}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}_{i}

where
\bigvee_{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ik}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i}
is a disjunctive normal form and
g_{l}
is some choice function
choosing one value
k
for each
i
.

Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ik}\vDash\bigvee_{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ik}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i}
holds we deduce by Lemma
C.3
the existence of derivations
D_{ik}
of
\Gamma\vdash^{X}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ik}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}_{i}
.

Moreover, since the
E_{l}
are canonical, by Lemma
D.11
we deduce that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{l}\vDash\bigwedge_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ig_{l}(i)}
.

Using
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\vDash\bigvee_{l}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{l}
, we can thus obtain a derivation of
\Gamma\vdash^{X}(\lambda x.t)u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
as follows (where
\mathfrak{M}=[\mathfrak{s}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{s}_{N}]
and
\mathfrak{s}=\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma
):

\left\{\hbox{\ignorespaces\minipage{345.0pt}$\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 37.40463pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 19.05132pt\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 32.71828pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$E_{l}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}\hbox{\vbox{\vskip 1.0pt}}\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{M}\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{l}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to122.56696pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\vdash^{X}\lambda x.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{l}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma)$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\left\{\hbox{\ignorespaces\minipage{345.0pt}$\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 8.41666pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 24.52692pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$D_{ig_{l}(i)}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}\hbox{\vbox{\vskip 1.0pt}}\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\vdash^{X}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ig_{l}(i)}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}_{i}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\ignorespaces$
\endminipage
}\right\}_{i}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{l}\vDash\bigwedge_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ig_{l}(i)}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to581.75317pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 244.94879pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\vdash^{X}(\lambda x.t)u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{l}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces$
\endminipage
}\right\}_{l}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash\bigvee_{l}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{l}

(\lor)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}(\lambda x.t)u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

∎

D.2.2.
Subject Permutative Expansion

The subject expansion property with respect to permutative reduction can be established by a direct inspection of permutative rules.

Proposition D.17.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
and
u\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}t
, then
\Gamma\vdash^{X}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
.

Proof.

As in the proof of Proposition
C.1
, if the typing derivation
D
of
t
ends by a
(\lor)
-rule, it suffices to establish the property for the immediate sub-derivations of
D
and then apply an instance of
(\lor)
-rule to the resulting derivations. So we will always suppose that the typing derivation of
t
does not end by a
(\lor)
-rule.

(
t\oplus_{a}^{i}t\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}t
):

From
\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
we deduce

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

(
\oplus l
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t\oplus_{a}^{i}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

(
\oplus r
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t\oplus_{a}^{i}t:\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b})\lor(\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b})

(
\vee
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t\oplus_{a}^{i}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

(
(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)\oplus_{a}^{i}v\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}t\oplus_{a}^{i}v
):

There are two possible sub-cases:

(1):

the type derivation is as follows:

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}

(
\oplus l
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t\oplus_{a}^{i}v:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

Then we deduce

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}

(
\oplus l
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t\oplus_{a}^{i}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}

(
\oplus l
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)\oplus_{a}^{i}v:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

(2):

the type derivation is as follows:

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}v:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\vDash\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}

(
\oplus l
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t\oplus_{a}^{i}v:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

Then we deduce

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}v:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}

(
\oplus l
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)\oplus_{a}^{i}v:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

(
t\oplus_{a}^{i}(u\oplus_{a}^{i}v)\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}t\oplus_{a}^{i}v
):

Similar to the case above.

(
\lambda x.(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}(\lambda x.t)\oplus_{a}^{i}(\lambda x.u)
):

There are two possible sub-cases, treated similarly. We only consider the first one:

(1):

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{M}\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma

(
\lambda
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}\lambda x.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}

(
\oplus l
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}(\lambda x.t)\oplus_{a}^{i}(\lambda x.u):{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma)

Then we deduce

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{M}\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}

(
\oplus l
)

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{M}\vdash^{X}t\oplus_{a}^{i}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma

(
\lambda
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}\lambda x.(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u):{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma)

(
(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)v\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}(tv)\oplus_{a}^{i}(uv)
):

There are two possible sub-cases, treated similarly.
We only consider the first one (with
\mathfrak{M}=[\mathfrak{s}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{s}_{n}]
:

(1):

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma)

\Big\{\Gamma\vdash^{X}v:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}_{i}\Big\}_{i}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}\land\left(\bigwedge_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\right)

(
@_{\cap}
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}tv:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}

(
\oplus l
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}(tv)\oplus_{a}^{i}(uv):{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma

Then we deduce

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}

(
\oplus l
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t\oplus_{a}^{i}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma)

\Big\{\Gamma\vdash^{X}v:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}_{i}\Big\}_{i}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime})\land\left(\bigwedge_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\right)

(
@_{\cap}
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)v:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma

(
t(u\oplus_{a}^{i}v)\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}(tu)\oplus_{a}^{i}(tv)
):

There are two sub-cases, treated similarly. We only consider the first one:

(1):

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma)

\Big\{\Gamma\vdash^{X}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}_{i}\Big\}_{i}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}\land\left(\bigwedge_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\right)

(
@_{\cap}
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}tu:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}

(
\oplus l
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}(tu)\oplus_{a}^{i}(tv):{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma

Then we deduce that

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma)

\left\{\hbox{\ignorespaces\minipage{345.0pt}$\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 11.6632pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\vdash^{X}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}_{i}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to146.38052pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt($\oplus l$)}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 20.52728pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\vdash^{X}u\oplus_{a}^{i}v:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}_{i}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces$
\endminipage
}\right\}_{i}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}\land\left(\bigwedge_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i})\right)

(
@_{\cap}
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t(u\oplus_{a}^{i}v):{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma

(
(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)\oplus_{b}^{j}v\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}(t\oplus_{b}^{j}v)\oplus_{a}^{i}(u\oplus_{b}^{j}v)
):

We suppose here
a\neq b
or
i<j
. There are four sub-cases, all treated similarly. We only consider the first one:

(1):

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{b}^{j}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}

(
\oplus l
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t\oplus_{b}^{j}v:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}

(
\oplus l
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}(t\oplus_{b}^{j}u)\oplus_{a}^{i}(u\oplus_{b}^{j}v):{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

Then we deduce

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}

(
\oplus l
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t\oplus_{a}^{i}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{b}^{j}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}

(
\oplus l
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)\oplus_{b}^{j}v:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

(
t\oplus_{b}^{j}(u\oplus_{a}^{i}v)\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}(t\oplus_{b}^{j}u)\oplus_{a}^{i}(t\oplus_{b}^{j}v)
):

Similar to the case above.

(
\nu b.(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}(\nu b.t)\oplus_{a}^{i}(\nu b.u)
):

We suppose
a\neq b
.
There are two sub-cases, treated similarly. We only consider the first one:

(1):

\left\{\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a,b\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{i}}\sigma\right\}_{i}

\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i})\geq s_{i}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}

(
\mu_{\Sigma}
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}\nu b.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}}\sigma

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}

(
\oplus l
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}(\nu b.t)\oplus_{a}^{i}(\nu b.u):{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}}\sigma

From
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime\prime}
we deduce by Lemma
C.3
the existence of derivations of

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{b\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{i}}\sigma
.
Then we can construct the following type derivation for
u
:

\left\{\hbox{\ignorespaces\minipage{345.0pt}$\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 14.37985pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 10.2811pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a,b\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{i}}\sigma$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to137.30705pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt($\oplus l$)}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a,b\}}t\oplus_{a}^{i}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{i}}\sigma$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\ignorespaces$
\endminipage
}\right\}_{i}

\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i})\geq s_{i}

(
\mu_{\Sigma}
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}\nu b.t\oplus_{a}^{i}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}}\sigma

(
\nu a.t\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}t
):

We can suppose that
a\notin\mathrm{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b})
, so
from
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}
we deduce

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
and since
\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top})\geq 1
,

\Gamma\vdash^{X}\nu a.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
, since
\mathfrak{s}=\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma=\mathbf{C}^{q\cdot 1}\sigma
.

(
\lambda x.\nu a.t\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}\nu a.\lambda x.t
):

\left\{\hbox{\ignorespaces\minipage{345.0pt}$\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 14.37985pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 11.80556pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{M}\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{i}}\sigma$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to159.4851pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt($\lambda$)}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}\lambda x.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{i}}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma)$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\ignorespaces$
\endminipage
}\right\}_{i}

\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i})\geq s_{i}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}

(
\mu_{\Sigma}
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}\nu a.\lambda x.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma)

from which we deduce

\{\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{M}\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{i}}\sigma\}_{i}

\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i})\geq s_{i}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}

(
\mu_{\Sigma}
)

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{M}\vdash^{X}\nu a.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}}\sigma

(
\lambda
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}\lambda x.\nu a.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma)

(
(\nu a.t)u\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}\nu a.(tu)
):

We have

\left\{\hbox{\ignorespaces\minipage{345.0pt}$\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 16.63084pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{i}}(\mathfrak{M}_{i}\Rightarrow\sigma)$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\left\{\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ij}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{t}_{ij}\right\}_{j}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{i}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\land\bigwedge_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ij}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to349.41768pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt($@_{\cap}$)}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 114.79727pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}tu:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{i}}\sigma$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces$
\endminipage
}\right\}_{i}

\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{i})\geq s_{i}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{\prime}

(
\mu_{\Sigma}
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}\nu a.tu:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}}\sigma

where
\mathfrak{M}_{i}=[\mathfrak{t}_{i1},\dots,\mathfrak{t}_{ip_{i}}]
.
Let
\mathfrak{M}
be the concatenation of
\mathfrak{M}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{M}_{k+1}
.
Since
\mathfrak{M}\preceq\mathfrak{M}_{i}
, we have
\mathbf{C}^{q}(\mathfrak{M}_{i}\Rightarrow\sigma)\preceq\mathbf{C}^{q}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma)
, so
from the derivations of
\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{i}}(\mathfrak{M}_{i}\Rightarrow\sigma)
, using Lemma
D.4
we deduce derivations of
\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma)
.
Moreover, from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{i}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{i}
,
using Lemma
D.3
, we deduce the existence of derivations of

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma)
, and from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{i}\vDash\bigwedge_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ij}
, since
\mathrm{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{i})\cap\mathrm{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ij})=\emptyset
, we deduce
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash\bigwedge_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ij}
.
We can thus construct a type derivation for
(\nu a.t)u
as follows:

\left\{\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma)\right\}_{i}

\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{i})\geq s_{i}

(
\mu_{\Sigma}
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}\nu a.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma)

\left\{\Gamma\vdash^{X,r_{i}}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ij}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{t}_{ij}\right\}_{i,j}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash\bigwedge_{ij}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{ij}

(
@_{\cap}
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}(\nu a.t)u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}q_{i}s_{i}}\sigma

Observe that this is the unique case in the proof in which we use intersection types in an essential way.

∎

D.3.
Completeness.

D.3.1.
Deterministic Completeness

The goal of this subsection is to establish Proposition
6.5
.

See
6.5

We need a few preliminary lemmas:

Lemma D.18.

For any closed term
t
:

(i.)

if
t
is a HNV, then
\vdash_{\lnot\vee}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{1}\sigma
holds for some type;

(ii.)

if
t
is a HNV and is
\rightarrowtriangle
-normal, then
\vdash_{\lnot\vee}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{1}\sigma
holds for some safe type.

Proof.

If
t=\lambda x_{1}\dots x_{n}.x_{i}u_{1}\dots u_{p}
, then
we can let
\sigma=\mathfrak{s}_{1}\Rightarrow\dots\Rightarrow\mathfrak{s}_{n}\Rightarrow o
, where
for
j\neq i
,
\mathfrak{s}_{j}=[\ ]
, and
\mathfrak{s}_{i}=\mathbf{C}^{1}[\mathbf{C}^{1}[\ ]\Rightarrow\dots\Rightarrow\mathbf{C}^{1}[\ ]\Rightarrow o]
.
The second claim is proved by induction on normal forms, using a similar construction.
∎

Lemma D.19.

If
\Gamma\vdash_{\lnot\vee}t[u/x]:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
is derivable without using
[]
, and
t
contains at least one occurrence of
x
, then
there exists
\mathfrak{M}\neq[]
such that

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{M}\vdash_{\lnot\vee}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
and

\Gamma\vdash_{\lnot\vee}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{M}
are derivable without using
[]
.

Proof.

It suffices to look at the proof of Proposition
D.16
, and observe that (1) since
u
occurs at least once in
t
,
\mathfrak{M}
cannot be
[]
, and (2)
since the original derivation has no occurrence of
(\vee)
, it must be canonical, and thus to construct the derivation of
\Gamma\vdash(\lambda x.t)u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
we do not need to make use of any application of (
\vee
).
∎

Using Lemma
D.18
together with the subject expansion property we can prove this first half of Proposition
6.5
. The second half comes from Corollary
E.16
.

Proof of Proposition
6.5
.

For (i.), if
t
is head normalizable, with head-normal form
u
, then by Lemma
B.8
the tree
RBT(t)=RBT(u)
is finite and its leaves are HNV
u_{1},\dots,u_{N}
. By Lemma
D.18

we can deduce
\vdash u_{i}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{1}\mathsf{hn}
; we can then climb up
RBT(t)
using the rules
(
\oplus l
), (
\oplus r
) and (
\mu_{\Sigma}
), hence proving
\vdash u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{1}\mathsf{hn}
, and finally

\vdash t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{1}\mathsf{hn}
by subject expansion.
The converse direction follows from Corollary
E.16
(i.).

The argument for (ii.) is similar.

For (iii.), let
t
be strongly normalizable, we argue by induction on the maximum length
N
of a reduction of
t
.
If
N=0
, then
t
is in normal form, so by an argument similar to (i.) we can deduce
\vdash_{\not\vee}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{1}\mathsf{n}
.
If
N>0
and
t\rightarrowtriangle u
, then by IH
\vdash_{\not\vee}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{1}\mathsf{n}
holds with a
[]
-free derivation, and
t=\mathtt{C}[(\lambda x.t^{\prime})t^{\prime\prime}]
,
u=\mathtt{C}[t^{\prime}[t^{\prime\prime}/x]]
. If
x
occurs in
t^{\prime}
, then we deduce from Lemma
D.19

that
\vdash_{\not\vee}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{1}\mathsf{n}
holds with a
[]
-derivation.
Otherwise, since
t^{\prime\prime}
must be strong normalizable, by IH
\vdash_{\not\vee}t^{\prime\prime}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{1}\mathsf{n}
, and we deduce, again by IH, that
\lambda x.t^{\prime}
can be typed by declaring
x:\mathbf{C}^{1}\mathsf{n}
. We can then conclude

\vdash_{\not\vee}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{1}\mathsf{n}
also in this case.

The converse claim follows from Corollary
E.16
(iii.)
∎

D.3.2.
Probabilistic Completeness

The goal of this subsection is to establish the completeness part of Theorem
6.6
:

See
6.6

More precisely, we will show the
\leq
-part of the equations above, while soundness (i.e. the
\geq
-part of the equations above) will follow from Theorem
6.1
and Theorem
6.3
, proved in the next section.

First, we need to study the structure of the trees
RBT(t)
(see Subsection
B.1
) further.

Definition D.20.

A
randomized multi-context

\mathtt{C}
is a term constructed from the grammar below

\mathtt{C}::=[\ ]_{i}\ (i\in\mathbb{N})\mid\mathtt{C}\oplus_{a}^{i}\mathtt{C}\mid\nu a.\mathtt{C}

where for each two occurrences
[\ ]_{i}
and
[\ ]_{j}
,
i\neq j
.
The rank
\mathsf{r}(\mathtt{C})
is the maximum
k
such that
[\ ]_{k}
occurs in
\mathtt{C}
.

For any randomized multi-context
\mathtt{C}
, we let
\mathtt{C}^{\lnot\nu}
be the randomized multi-context obtained by deleting all occurrences of
\nu
-binders.

If
\mathtt{C}
is a randomized multi-context of rank
k
, for all terms
t_{1},\dots,t_{k}
, we let
\mathtt{C}[t_{1},\dots,t_{k}]
indicate the term obtained by replacing each hole
[\ ]_{i}
in
\mathtt{C}
by
t_{i}
.

An
initial segment

T
of
RBT(t)
, noted
T\sqsubset RBT(t)
is any sub-tree of
RBT(t)
which contains its root.

Observe that a finite initial segment
T\sqsubset RBT(t)
is the same thing as a term of the form

\mathtt{C}_{T}[u_{1},\dots,u_{k}]
for some randomized multi-context
\mathtt{C}_{T}
.

Lemma D.21.

For any term
t
and HNF
u_{1},\dots,u_{k}
, if there exist randomized paths from
t
to
u_{1},\dots,u_{k}
, then there exists
N\in\mathbb{N}
, a randomized multi-context
\mathtt{C}
of rank
N+k
and terms
t_{1},\dots,t_{N}
such that

t\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}^{*}\mathtt{C}[u_{1},\dots,u_{k},t_{1},\dots,t_{N}]

and
RBT(\mathtt{C}[u_{1},\dots,u_{k},t_{1},\dots,t_{N}])\sqsubseteq RBT(t)
.

Proof.

We argue by induction on the maximum length
K
of the randomized paths considered.
If
K=0
,
k=1
and
t=u
, so we just let
N=0
and
\mathtt{C}[\ ]=[\ ]
.

If
K\geq 1
, then in a finite number of reduction steps we get
t\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}^{*}\nu a.T
, where
T
is a
({T},a)
-tree such that for each
i=1,\dots,k
, there exists
v_{i}\in\mathsf{supp}(T)
with a randomized path from
v_{i}
to
u_{i}
of length
\leq K-1
; observe that we can write
T
as
\mathtt{D}[v_{1},\dots,v_{q}]
for some randomized multi-context
\mathtt{C}
.

For any
j=1,\dots,q
, let
I_{j}
be the set of values
i\leq k
such that there is a randomized path from
v_{j}
to
u_{i}
. The
I_{j}
are disjoint sets with
\bigcup_{j}I_{j}=\{1,\dots,k\}
.
By the I.H. we then deduce that there exist randomized multicontexts
\mathtt{C}_{j}
or rank
N_{j}+|I_{j}|
and terms
w_{j1},\dots,w_{jN_{j}}
such that

v_{j}\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}^{*}\mathtt{C}[u_{i_{1}},\dots,u_{i_{M_{j}}},w_{j1},\dots,w_{jN_{j}}]
, where
I_{j}=\{i_{1},\dots,i_{M_{j}}\}
. We now have that

\displaystyle t\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}^{*}\nu a.T=\nu a.\mathtt{C}[v_{1},\dots,v_{q}]\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}^{*}\nu a.\mathtt{D}[\mathtt{C}_{1}[\vec{u},\vec{w}],\dots,\mathtt{C}_{q}[\vec{u},\vec{w}]]

so we can let
N=\sum_{j}N_{j}
,

\mathtt{C}=\nu a.\mathtt{D}[\mathtt{C}_{1},\dots,\mathtt{C}_{q}]
and
t_{1},\dots,t_{N}=w_{11},\dots,w_{qM_{q}}
.
∎

Lemma D.22.

For any randomized multi-context
\mathtt{C}
with
n
bound names
a_{1},\dots,a_{n}
,

(3)

\nu a_{1}.\dots.\nu a_{n}.\mathtt{C}^{\lnot\nu}\ \rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}^{*}\ \mathtt{C}

Proof.

We will prove the following fact: for all terms
\nu b.t,t_{1},\dots,t_{k}
, it
\mathtt{C}
has no
\nu
-binders, then

(4)

\nu b.\mathtt{C}[t_{1},t_{2},\dots,t_{k},t]\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}^{*}\mathtt{C}[t_{1},\dots,t_{k},\nu b.t]

From this fact we can deduce the main claim as follows: let
b
be a bound variable in
\mathtt{C}
whose binder
\nu
has no other binder in its scope; then

\mathtt{C}
splits as
\mathtt{C}[t_{0},t_{1},\dots,t_{k}]=\mathtt{E}[t_{i_{1}},\dots,t_{i_{p}},\nu b.\mathtt{D}[t_{j_{1}},\dots,t_{j_{q}}]]
, where

I=\{i_{1},\dots,i_{p}\},J=\{j_{1},\dots,j_{q}\}\subseteq\{1,\dots,k+1\}
satisfy
I\cap J=\emptyset
and
p+q=\mathsf{r}(\mathtt{C})
(we are here supposing that
r(\mathtt{C})\geq 1
, because otherwise the claim is trivial).

Then, by (
4
) we deduce that
\nu b.\mathtt{E}^{\lnot\nu}[t_{i_{1}},\dots,t_{i_{p}},\mathtt{D}[t_{j_{1}},\dots,t_{j_{q}}]]\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}\mathtt{E}^{\lnot\nu}[t_{i_{1}},\dots,t_{i_{p}},\nu b.\mathtt{D}[t_{j_{1}},\dots,t_{j_{q}}]]
; by arguing in this way on all binders
\nu
we finally obtain (
3
).

We prove (
4
) by induction on the construction of
\mathtt{C}
:

\bullet

if
\mathtt{C}=[\ ]_{i}
, then the claim trivially holds;

\bullet

if
\mathtt{C}=\mathtt{C}_{1}\oplus_{a}^{j}\mathtt{C}_{2}
, then
[\ ]_{k+1}
occurs in either
\mathtt{C}_{1}
or
\mathtt{C}_{2}
, and not both; let us say
[\ ]_{k+1}
occurs in
\mathtt{C}_{1}
; we then have that

\displaystyle\nu b.\mathtt{C}[t_{1},\dots,t_{k},t]

\displaystyle=\nu b.\mathtt{C}_{1}[t_{i_{1}},\dots,t_{i_{p}},t]\oplus_{a}^{j}\mathtt{C}_{2}[t_{j_{1}},\dots,t_{j_{q}}]

\displaystyle\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}\big(\nu b.\mathtt{C}_{1}[t_{i_{1}},\dots,t_{i_{p}},t]\big)\oplus_{a}^{i}\big(\nu b.\mathtt{C}_{2}[t_{j_{1}},\dots,t_{j_{q}}]\big)

\displaystyle\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}\big(\nu b.\mathtt{C}_{1}[t_{i_{1}},\dots,t_{i_{p}},t]\big)\oplus_{a}^{i}\mathtt{C}_{2}[t_{j_{1}},\dots,t_{j_{q}}]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tiny\text{I.H.}}}{{\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}^{*}}}\mathtt{C}_{1}[t_{i_{1}},\dots,t_{i_{p}},\nu b.t]\oplus_{a}^{i}\mathtt{C}_{2}[t_{j_{1}},\dots,t_{j_{q}}]=\mathtt{C}[t_{1},\dots,t_{k},\nu b.t]

∎

Still one technical lemmas before proceeding to the proof of Theorem
6.6
.

Lemma D.23.

Let
N,K\geq 1
,
X=\{a_{1},\dots,a_{K}\}
be distinct names, and for all
i\in N
and
j\in K
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{ij}
be a conjunction of literals of name
a_{j}
such that for all
i\neq i^{\prime}
:

a.

for all
k\leq K
, if
\bigwedge_{j=1}^{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{ij}\neq\bigwedge_{j=1}^{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i^{\prime}j}
, then

\left(\bigwedge_{j=1}^{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{ij}\right)\land\left(\bigwedge_{j=1}^{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{ij}\right)\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bot}
;

b.

\left(\bigwedge_{j=1}^{K}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{ij}\right)\land\left(\bigwedge_{j=1}^{K}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i^{\prime}j}\right)\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bot}
.

Suppose
\Gamma\vdash^{X,q_{i}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\land\left(\bigwedge_{j=1}^{K}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{ij}\right)\rightarrowtail\sigma
is derivable for all
i\in\{1,\dots,N\}
. Then there exists a derivation of
\Gamma\vdash^{\emptyset}\nu a_{1}.\dots.\nu a_{K}.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{r}\sigma
, where
r=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(q_{i}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{K}\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{ij})\right)
.

Proof.

We argue by induction on
K
. If
K=1
then
i\neq i^{\prime}
implies
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i^{\prime}}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bot}
, so we can conclude as follows:

\left\{\hbox{\ignorespaces\minipage{345.0pt}$\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 9.23111pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 39.29352pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$D_{i}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}\hbox{\vbox{\vskip 1.0pt}}\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\vdash^{\{a\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{i}}\sigma$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\ignorespaces$
\endminipage
}\right\}_{i=1,\dots,N}

(\mu_{\Sigma})

\Gamma\vdash^{\emptyset}\nu a.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i=1}^{N}q_{i}\cdot\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i})}\sigma

If
K>1
, then
for all
i,i^{\prime}\in\{1,\dots,N\}
, let
i\sim i^{\prime}
if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{iK}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i^{\prime}K}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bot}

and for all
j\in\{1,\dots K-1\}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{ij}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i^{\prime}j}
.
Let
\mathsf{Cl}(N_{\sim})
indicate the set of cliques of the relation
\sim
, and for each
U\in\mathsf{Cl}(N_{\sim})
, fix some
i_{U}\in U
. Then we claim that the following equation holds:

(5)

\sum_{i=1}^{N}q_{i}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{K}\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{ij})=\sum_{U\in\mathsf{Cl}_{N_{\sim}}}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{K-1}\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i_{U}j})\right)\cdot\left(\sum_{i\in U}q_{i}\cdot\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{iK})\right)

Indeed we can compute

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(q_{i}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{K}\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{ij})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{U\in\mathsf{Cl}_{N_{\sim}}}\sum_{i\in U}\left(q_{i}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{K}\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{ij})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{U\in\mathsf{Cl}_{N_{\sim}}}\sum_{i\in U}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{K-1}\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i_{U}j})\right)\cdot\Big(q_{i}\cdot\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{iK})\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{U\in\mathsf{Cl}_{N_{\sim}}}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{K-1}\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i_{U}j})\right)\cdot\left(\sum_{i\in U}q_{i}\cdot\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{iK})\right)

Let us now show how to construct, for all
U\in\mathsf{Cl}_{N_{\sim}}
, a derivation
D_{U}
of

\Gamma\vdash^{\{a_{1},\dots,a_{K-1}\}}\nu a_{K}.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\land\left(\prod_{j=1}^{K-1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i_{U}j}\right)\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{U}}\sigma
, where
q_{U}=\sum_{i\in U}q_{i}\cdot\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{iK})
:

D_{U}\quad=\quad\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 22.48305pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\left\{\hbox{\ignorespaces\minipage{345.0pt}$\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 8.41666pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 66.41428pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$D_{i_{U}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}\hbox{\vbox{\vskip 1.0pt}}\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\land\left(\bigwedge_{j=1}^{K-1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i_{U}j}\right)\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i_{U}K}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{i}}\sigma$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\ignorespaces$
\endminipage
}\right\}_{j\in U}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to382.13402pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt$(\mu_{\Sigma})$}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 101.18652pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\vdash^{X-\{a_{K}\}}\nu a_{K}.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\land\left(\bigwedge_{j=1}^{K-1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i_{U}j}\right)\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{U}}\sigma$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{U}=\bigwedge_{j=1}^{K-1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i_{U}j}
,
t^{\prime}=\nu a_{K}.t
and observe that we now have derivations
D_{U}
of

\Gamma\vdash^{\{a_{1},\dots,a_{K-1}\},q_{U}}t^{\prime}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{U}\rightarrowtail\sigma
, where for all

U\neq V
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{U}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{V}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bot}
.
Moreover, for all
U\neq V
, from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{U}\not\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{V}
we deduce by condition (a.) that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{U}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{V}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bot}
; finally condition (b.) for the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{U}
follows immediately from condition (b.) for the
\bigwedge_{j=1}^{K}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{ij}
.

Hence, by applying the induction hypothesis, we deduce the existence of a derivation of

\Gamma\vdash^{\emptyset}\nu X.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma
, where

\displaystyle q

\displaystyle=\sum_{U\in\mathsf{Cl}_{N_{\sim}}}q_{U}\cdot\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{U}))=\sum_{U\in\mathsf{Cl}_{N_{\sim}}}\left(q_{U}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{K-1}\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i_{U}j}))\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{U\in\mathsf{Cl}_{N_{\sim}}}\left(\sum_{i\in U}q_{i}\cdot\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{iK})\right)\cdot\left(\prod_{j=1}^{K-1}\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{i_{U}j}))\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:cliques}}}{{=}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(q_{i}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{K}\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{ij})\right)

so we are done.
∎

The technical lemma just proved can be used to establish Proposition
D.24
, i.e. the admissibility of the generalized counting rule
(\mu^{*})
.

Proposition D.24.

The following generalized counting rule is derivable in
\mathbf{C}\lambda_{\to,\cap}
:

\Gamma\vdash^{\{a_{1},\dots,a_{K}\}}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma

(\mu^{*})

\Gamma\vdash^{\emptyset}\nu a_{1}.\dots.\nu a_{K}.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q\cdot\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b})}\sigma

Proof.

First, let us transform
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}
into a disjunctive normal form of the form
\bigvee_{i=1}^{N}\bigwedge_{j=1}^{K}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{ij}
, where each
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{ij}
is a conjunction of literals of name
a_{j}
. Now, as it was done in the proof of Lemma
D.13
, the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{ij}
can be turned into conjunctions of literals
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ij}
, with
i\leq N^{\prime}
, for some
N^{\prime}\geq N
, and
j\leq K
, where for all
i\neq i^{\prime}
and
j,j^{\prime}
, either
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ij}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ij}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bot}
, and

\bigvee_{i=1}^{N^{\prime}}\bigwedge_{j=1}^{K}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ij}\equiv\bigvee_{i=1}^{N}\bigwedge_{j=1}^{K}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}_{ij}
.

Observe that the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ij}
now satisfy conditions (a.) and (b.) of Lemma
D.23
.
Moreover, by letting
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}_{i}=\bigwedge_{j=1}^{K}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}e}_{ij}
, from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}_{i}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}
we deduce that for all
i=1,\dots,N^{\prime}
,

\Gamma\vdash^{\{a_{1},\dots,a_{K}\},q}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}_{i}\rightarrowtail\sigma
is derivable.
Hence, by applying the Lemma, we can conclude that

\Gamma\vdash^{\emptyset,r}\nu a_{1}.\dots.\nu a_{K}.t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\rightarrowtail\sigma
, where

r=\sum_{i=1}^{N^{\prime}}q\cdot\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}_{i})=q\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{N^{\prime}}\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f}_{i})=q\cdot\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b})
.
∎

We can finally turn to the proof of the completeness part Theorem
6.6
.

Proposition D.25.

For any closed term
t
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}(t)

\displaystyle\leq\sup\{q\mid\ \vdash t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\mathsf{hn}\}

\displaystyle\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}(t)

\displaystyle\leq\sup\{q\mid\ \vdash t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\mathsf{n}\}

Proof.

First observe that (with the notations from Subsection
B.1
)

\displaystyle\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}(t)

\displaystyle=\sup\left\{\sum_{\pi:v\mapsto w\in\mathrm{HNV}}\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{v}_{\pi})\mid t\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{h}}^{*}v\right\}

\displaystyle=\sup\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}^{u}_{\pi_{i}})\mid N\in\mathbb{N},u_{1},\dots,u_{N}\in\mathrm{HNV},\ \pi_{1}:v\mapsto u_{1},\dots,\pi_{N}:t\mapsto u_{N},t\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{h}}^{*}v\right\}

so it is enough to show that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, term
v
such that
t\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{h}}^{*}v
and terms
u_{1},\dots,u_{n}\in\mathrm{HNV}
together with randomized paths
\pi_{i}:v\mapsto u_{i}
,
we can prove that
\vdash t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\mathsf{hn}
is derivable, with
q=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{\pi_{i}})
.
Notice that, by subject expansion, it suffices to show
\vdash v:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\mathsf{hn}
.

Let
\pi_{1}:v\mapsto u_{1},\dots,\pi_{k}:t\mapsto u_{k}
be randomized paths, with
u_{1},\dots,u_{n}\in\mathrm{HNV}
.
By Lemma
D.21
there exists
N
, terms
v_{1},\dots,v_{N}
and a randomized multi-context
\mathtt{C}
of rank
k+N
such that
v\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}^{*}\mathtt{C}[u_{1},\dots,u_{k},v_{1},\dots,v_{N}]
.
Moreover, by Lemma
D.22
there exists a permutative reduction

\nu a_{1}\dots\nu a_{K}.\mathtt{C}^{\lnot\nu}[u_{1},\dots,u_{k},v_{1},\dots,v_{N}]\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}^{*}\mathtt{C}[u_{1},\dots,u_{k},v_{1},\dots,v_{N}]
.

Let
v^{*}
be the term
\mathtt{C}^{\lnot\nu}[u_{1},\dots,u_{k},v_{1},\dots,v_{N}]
.
For each HNV
u_{i}
, by Lemma
D.18
, we have that
\vdash u_{i}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{1}\mathsf{hn}
, so in particular

\vdash^{\{a_{1},\dots,a_{K}\},1}u_{i}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{1}\mathsf{hn}
is derivable; using this fact one can construct by induction on
\mathtt{C}
derivations
D_{i}
of

\vdash^{\{a_{1},\dots,a_{K}\}}v^{*}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{\pi_{i}}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{1}\mathsf{hn}

(using the fact that
RBT(\mathtt{C}[u_{1},\dots,u_{k},v_{1},\dots,v_{N}])\sqsubseteq RBT(t)
)
:

\bullet

if
\mathtt{C}^{\lnot\nu}=[\ ]_{i}
, then
k=1
,
u^{*}=u
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{\pi_{1}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}
; then by the hypothesis we have

\vdash v^{*}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{1}\mathsf{hn}
;

\bullet

if
\mathtt{C}^{\lnot\nu}=\mathtt{C}_{1}\oplus_{a}^{l}\mathtt{C}_{2}
, then
u^{*}=\mathtt{C}_{1}[u_{i_{1}},\dots,u_{i_{p}},v_{i^{\prime}_{1}},\dots,v_{i^{\prime}_{r}}]\oplus_{a}^{l}\mathtt{C}_{2}[u_{j_{1}},\dots,u_{j_{q}},v_{j^{\prime}_{1}},\dots,v_{j^{\prime}_{s}}]
, where
p+q=k
,
r+s=N
and

I\cap J=\{i_{1},\dots,i_{p}\}\cap\{j_{1},\dots,j_{q}\}=\emptyset
,

I^{\prime}\cap J^{\prime}=\{i^{\prime}_{1},\dots,i^{\prime}_{r}\}\cap\{j^{\prime}_{1},\dots,j^{\prime}_{s}\}=\emptyset
.
Moreover, we have that for all
m\in I
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{\pi_{l}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{l}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{\pi^{\prime}_{m}}
, where
\pi^{\prime}_{m}
is the path of
u_{m}
in
\mathtt{C}_{1}
; similarly, for all
m\in J
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{\pi_{m}}=\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{l}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{\pi^{\prime}_{m}}
, where
\pi^{\prime}_{m}
is the path of
u_{l}
in
\mathtt{C}_{2}
.

By the induction hypothesis we then have derivations of
\vdash^{\{a_{1},\dots,a_{K}\}}\mathtt{C}_{1}[u_{i_{1}},\dots,u_{i_{p}},v_{i^{\prime}_{1}},\dots,v_{i^{\prime}_{r}}]:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{\pi^{\prime}_{m}}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{1}\mathsf{hn}
and

\vdash^{\{a_{1},\dots,a_{K}\}}\mathtt{C}_{2}[u_{j_{1}},\dots,u_{j_{q}},v_{j^{\prime}_{1}},\dots,v_{j^{\prime}_{s}}]:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{\pi^{\prime}_{m}}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{1}\mathsf{hn}
.
Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{\pi_{i}}
is either

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{l}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{\pi^{\prime}_{i}}
or
\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{l}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{\pi^{\prime}_{i}}
,
on
i
, we obtain then in any case a derivation of

\vdash^{\{a_{1},\dots,a_{K}\}}v^{*}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{\pi_{i}}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{1}\mathsf{hn}
using either the
(\oplus l)
- or the
(\oplus r)
-rule.

Using the derivations
D_{i}
we thus obtain a derivation of
\vdash^{\emptyset}\nu a_{1}.\dots.\nu a_{K}.v^{*}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{\pi_{i}})}\mathsf{hn}
by applying the derivable rule
(\mu^{*})
(Corollary
D.24
) as shown below:

\left\{\hbox{\ignorespaces\minipage{345.0pt}$\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 9.23111pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 46.5343pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$D_{i}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}\hbox{\vbox{\vskip 1.0pt}}\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\vdash^{\{a_{1},\dots,a_{K}\}}v^{*}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{\pi_{i}}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{1}\mathsf{hn}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\ignorespaces$
\endminipage
}\right\}_{i=1,\dots,k}

\bigvee_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{\pi_{i}}\vDash\bigvee_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{\pi_{i}}

(\lor)

\vdash^{\{a_{1},\dots,a_{K}\}}v^{*}:\bigvee_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{\pi_{i}}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{1}\mathsf{hn}

(\mu^{*})

\vdash^{\emptyset}\nu a_{1}.\dots.\nu a_{K}.v^{*}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\mu(\bigvee_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{\pi_{i}})}\mathsf{hn}

Since
\mu(\bigvee_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{\pi_{i}})=\sum_{i}\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{\pi_{i}})
, using subject reduction and subject expansion we can finally deduce

\vdash^{\emptyset}v:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{i}\mu({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{\pi_{i}})}\mathsf{hn}
.

For the case of
\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}(t)
, we establish the result first for
\mathrm{NF}(t)
, arguing by induction on normal forms with an argument similar to the one above, exploiting the fact that if

q_{i}=\sup S_{i}
, then

\prod_{i=1}^{k}q_{i}=\sup\{\prod_{i=1}^{k}s_{i}\mid s_{i}\in S_{i}\}
.
∎

Appendix E
Normalization Results

In this section we establish a few normalization results for
\mathbf{C}\lambda^{\{\}}_{\to}
and
\mathbf{C}\lambda_{\to,\cap}
.
All results are based on some variant of the standard reducibility predicates technique.

First, we prove Theorem
5.3
by adapting a result from
[
16
]
to the language
\Lambda^{\{\}}_{\mathsf{{PE}}}
.
We then turn to probabilistic normalization. In Subsection
E.2
we develop a theory of reducibility candidates for probabilistic head normal forms and probabilistic normal forms for
\Lambda_{\mathsf{{PE}}}
and for a general family of types which comprises both those of
\mathbf{C}\lambda^{\{\}}_{\to}
an
\mathbf{C}\lambda_{\to,\cap}
. This will allows us to establish the normalization theorem for
\mathbf{C}\lambda_{\to,\cap}
, but will also provide most of the steps needed to establish the normalization theorem for
\mathbf{C}\lambda^{\{\}}_{\to}
. In Subsection
E.3
we discuss reducibility candidates for
\Lambda^{\{\}}_{\mathsf{{PE}}}
, by adapting most of results from the previous subsection, and we complete the proof of Theorem
6.1
.

E.1.
Deterministic Normalization.

E.1.1.
Deterministic Normalization of
\mathbf{C}\lambda^{\{\}}_{\to}
.

In this section we adapt the proof of [
[
16
]
, Theorem 1] to
\mathbf{C}\lambda^{\{\}}_{\to}
, in order to prove Theorem
5.3
:

See
5.3

We introduce a simply typed
\lambda
-calculus
\lambda_{\to}^{\{\}}
for
\Lambda^{\{\}}_{\mathsf{{PE}}}
and we show that typable terms are strongly normalizable under
\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}}
. Simple types are defined by the grammar below

\sigma::=o\mid\sigma\Rightarrow\sigma

A judgement of
\lambda_{\to}^{\{\}}
is of the form
\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t:\sigma
, where
\theta
is a
list
of names and
\mathsf{FN}(t)\subseteq\theta
(observe that we consider here lists and not sets).
The rules of
\lambda_{\to}^{\{\}}
are illustrated in Fig.
16
.

The following is easily established by inspecting all reduction rules:

Proposition E.1 (subject reduction).

If
\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t:\sigma
and
t\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}}u
, then
\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}u:\sigma
.

For any type
\mathfrak{s}
of
\mathbf{C}\lambda^{\{\}}_{\to}
, let us define the corresponding simple type
|\mathfrak{s}|
by letting

|o|=o
,
|\mathfrak{s}\Rightarrow\sigma|=|\mathfrak{s}|\Rightarrow|\sigma|
and
|\mathbf{C}^{q}\mathfrak{s}|=|\mathfrak{s}|
. The following is easily checked by induction:

Proposition E.2.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{X}_{\lnot\vee}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
holds
\mathbf{C}\lambda^{\{\}}_{\to}
, then

|\Gamma|\vdash^{\theta}t:|\mathfrak{s}|
is derivable in
\lambda_{\to}^{\{\}}
, for any suitable
\theta
.

\Gamma,x:\sigma\vdash^{\theta}x:\sigma

\Gamma,x:\sigma\vdash^{\theta}t:\tau

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}\lambda x.t:\sigma\Rightarrow\tau

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t:\sigma\Rightarrow\tau

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}u:\sigma

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}tu:\tau

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t:\sigma

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}u:\sigma

a\in\theta

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t\oplus_{a}^{i}u:\sigma

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t:\sigma

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta-\{a\}}\nu a.t:\sigma

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t:\sigma\Rightarrow\tau

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}u:\sigma

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}\{t\}u:\tau

.

Lemma E.3.

(i.)

if

,

occurs in

Proof.

(i.)

Same argument as [

, Lemma 24],
observing that a

-redex in
(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)v_{1}\dots v_{n}
can only occur inside one of
t,u,v_{1},\dots,v_{n}
.

(ii.)

Same argument as [
[
16
]
, Lemma 25], again observing that a
\{\}
-redex in
(\nu a.t)v_{1}\dots v_{n}
can only occur inside one of
t,u,v_{1},\dots,v_{n}
.

(iii.)

Immediate.

(iv.)

Same argument as [
[
16
]
, Lemma 27], again observing that a
\{\}
-redex in

(\lambda x.t)uv_{1}\dots v_{n}

can only occur inside one of
t,u,v_{1},\dots,v_{n}
.

∎

Lemma E.4.

Let
t^{\bullet}
be obtained from
t
by deleting a finite number of
\nu
-binders
\nu a_{1},\dots,\nu a_{k}
.
If
t\in\mathrm{SN}^{\theta}
, then
t^{\bullet}\in\mathrm{SN}^{\theta^{\prime}}
, for any suitable
\theta^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We argue by induction on
|t|+\mathrm{sn}^{\theta}(t)
:

\bullet

if
t=x
or
t=y
, the claim is immediate;

\bullet

if
t=\lambda y.t^{\prime}
, the claim follows by IH;

\bullet

if
t=t_{1}t_{2}
, then
t^{\bullet}=t_{1}^{\bullet}t_{2}^{\bullet}
; let us consider all possible reductions of
t^{\bullet}
:

\bullet

a reduction of either
t_{1}^{\bullet}
or
t_{2}^{\bullet}
, in which case we apply the IH;

\bullet

a reduction
(\lambda y.u^{\bullet})t_{2}^{\bullet}\rightarrowtriangle_{\beta}u^{\bullet}[t_{2}^{\bullet}/y]
, where
t_{1}=\lambda y.u
, in which case we also apply the IH;

\bullet

a reduction of the form
u^{\bullet}t_{2}^{\bullet}\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}}v^{\bullet}
, where
t_{1}=\nu a.u
, then
t_{1}t_{2}\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}\{\}}\nu a.ut_{2}
, so we can apply the IH to
ut_{2}
.

\bullet

if
t=\{t_{1}\}t_{2}
, then
t^{\bullet}=\{t_{1}^{\bullet}\}t_{2}^{\bullet}
; then a reduction is either a reduction of either
t_{1}^{\bullet}
or
t_{2}^{\bullet}
, in which case we apply the IH, either a
\eqref{eq:e1},\eqref{eq:e2}
-reduction, in which case we also apply the IH, or a reduction
of the form
\{t_{1}^{\bullet}\}\nu a.u^{\bullet}\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}\{\}}\nu a.t_{1}^{\bullet}u^{\bullet}
, where
t_{2}=\nu a.u
, in which case we also have
\{t_{1}\}t_{2}\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}\{\}}\nu a.t_{1}u
, so we can apply the IH.

\bullet

if
t=t_{1}\oplus_{a}^{i}t_{2}
, then a reduction of
t^{\bullet}
is either a reduction of
t_{1}^{\bullet}
or
t_{2}^{\bullet}
, in which case we apply the IH, or a reduction of the form

t_{1}^{\bullet}\oplus_{a}^{i}(u^{\bullet}\oplus_{b}^{j}v^{\bullet})\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}\{\}}(t_{1}^{\bullet}\oplus_{a}^{i}u^{\bullet})\oplus_{b}^{j}(t_{1}^{\bullet}\oplus_{a}^{i}v^{\bullet})
, where
t_{2}=v\oplus_{a}^{i}w
, in which case
t\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}\{\}}(t_{1}\oplus_{a}^{i}u)\oplus_{b}^{j}(t_{2}\oplus_{a}^{i}v)
, so we can apply the IH;

\bullet

if
t=\nu b.u
, then if
t^{\bullet}=u^{\bullet}
, we can argue by the IH; if
t^{\bullet}=\nu b.u^{\bullet}
, then a reduction of
t^{\bullet}
is either a reduction of
u^{\bullet}
, in which case we can apply the IH, or a permutation
\nu b.u_{1}^{\bullet}\oplus_{a}^{i}u_{2}^{\bullet}\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}\{\}}(\nu b.u_{1}^{\bullet})\oplus_{a}^{i}(\nu b.u_{2}^{\bullet})
, where
u=u_{1}\oplus_{a}^{i}u_{2}
, in which case
t\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}\{\}}(\nu b.u_{1})\oplus_{a}^{i}(\nu b.u_{2})
so we can apply the IH.

∎

Lemma E.5.

(i.)

If
(\nu a.tu)v_{1}\dots v_{n}\in\mathrm{SN}^{\theta}
, then
\{t\}(\nu a.u)v_{1}\dots v_{n}\in\mathrm{SN}^{\theta}
.

(ii.)

If
t[\nu a.u/x]v_{1}\dots v_{n}\in\mathrm{SN}^{\theta}
, then
t[u/x]v_{1}\dots v_{n}\in\mathrm{SN}^{\theta^{\prime}}
, for any suitable
\theta^{\prime}
.

(iii.)

If
(tu)v_{1}\dots v_{n}\in\mathrm{SN}^{\theta}
, then
(\{t\}u)v_{1}\dots v_{n}\in\mathrm{SN}^{\theta}
.

Proof.

(i.)

We argue by induction on
|t|+\mathrm{sn}^{\theta}(t)+\mathrm{sn}^{\theta}(v)+\sum_{i}\mathrm{sn}^{\theta}(v_{i})
.

A reduction of
\{t\}(\nu a.u)v_{1}\dots v_{n}
can only be a reduction in either of
t,u,v_{1}\dots,v_{n}
, a reduction of the form

\{t_{1}\oplus_{a}^{i}t_{2}\}(\nu a.u)v_{1}\dots v_{n}\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}\{\}}(\{t_{1}\}(\nu a.u)v_{1}\dots v_{n})\oplus_{a}^{i}(\{t_{1}\}(\nu a.u)v_{1}\dots v_{n})
, or a reduction

\{t\}(\nu a.u)v_{1}\dots v_{n}\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}\{\}}(\nu a.tu)v_{1}\dots v_{n}
. In the first case we can use the IH; in the second case by the IH we deduce
(\{t_{i}\}(\nu a.u)v_{1}\dots v_{n})\in\mathrm{SN}^{\theta}
and we can conclude by Lemma
E.3
(i.). In the third case we conclude by the hypothesis.

(ii.)

Immediate application of Lemma
E.4
.

(iii.)

We argue by induction on
n+|t|+|u|+\mathrm{sn}^{\theta}(t)+\mathrm{sn}^{\theta}(u)+\sum_{i}\mathrm{sn}^{\theta}(v_{i})
.

A reduction of
(\{t\}u)v_{1}\dots v_{n}
is of one of the following forms:

\bullet

a reduction of
t
or of
u
,
v_{1}
,…,
v_{n}
in which case we argue by the IH;

\bullet

a reduction of the form
(\{t\}\nu a.u^{\prime})v_{1}\dots v_{n}\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}\{\}}(\nu a.tu^{\prime})v_{1}\dots v_{n}
, where
u=\nu a.u^{\prime}
; then by Lemma
E.4
, from
(tu)v_{1}\dots v_{n}\in\mathrm{SN}^{\theta}
we deduce
(tu^{\prime})v_{1}\dots v_{n}\in\mathrm{SN}^{X\cup\{a\}}
, and by Lemma
E.3
(ii.),

(\nu a.tu^{\prime})v_{1}\dots v_{n}\in\mathrm{SN}^{\theta}
;

\bullet

a reduction of the form
(\{t_{1}\oplus_{a}^{i}t_{2}\}u)v_{1}\dots v_{n}\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}\{\}}((\{t_{1}\}u)\oplus_{a}^{i}(\{t_{2}\}u))v_{1}\dots v_{n}
,where
t=t_{1}\oplus_{a}^{i}t_{2}
; then by IH

(\{t_{i}\}u)v_{1}\dots v_{n}\in\mathrm{SN}^{\theta}
, so we conclude
((\{t_{1}\}u)\oplus_{a}^{i}(\{t_{2}\}u))v_{1}\dots v_{n}
by Lemma
E.3
(i.).

\bullet

a reduction of the form
(\{t\}(u_{1}\oplus_{a}^{i}u_{2}))v_{1}\dots v_{n}\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}\{\}}((\{t\}u_{1})\oplus_{a}^{i}(\{t\}u_{2}))v_{1}\dots v_{n}
;
then by IH

(\{t\}u_{i})v_{1}\dots v_{n}\in\mathrm{SN}^{\theta}
, so we conclude
((\{t\}u_{1})\oplus_{a}^{i}(\{t\}u_{2}))v_{1}\dots v_{n}
by Lemma
E.3
(i.).

\bullet

a reduction of the form
((\{t\}u)v_{1}\dots v_{k})(v_{k+1}^{1}\oplus_{a}^{i}v_{k+1}^{2})v_{k+2}\dots v_{n}\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}\{\}}((\{t\}u)v_{1}\dots v_{k}v_{k+1}^{1})\oplus_{a}^{i}((\{t\}u)v_{1}\dots v_{k}v_{k+1}^{2})v_{k+2}\dots v_{n}
; then by IH

(\{t\}u)v_{1}\dots v_{k}v_{k+1}^{1}\in\mathrm{SN}^{\theta}
,

(\{t\}u)v_{1}\dots v_{k}v_{k+1}^{2}\in\mathrm{SN}^{\theta}
, and by Lemma
E.3
(i.) we conclude

((\{t\}u)v_{1}\dots v_{k}v_{k+1}^{1})\oplus_{a}^{i}((\{t\}u)v_{1}\dots v_{k}v_{k+1}^{2})v_{k+2}\dots v_{n}\in\mathrm{SN}^{\theta}
.

∎

The family of reducibility predicates
\mathrm{Red}_{\sigma}^{\theta}
of
\lambda_{\to}^{\{\}}
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Red}^{\theta}_{o}

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{SN}^{\theta}

\displaystyle\mathrm{Red}^{\theta}_{\sigma\Rightarrow\tau}

\displaystyle:=\{t\in\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu,\{\}}^{X}\mid\forall u\in\mathrm{Red}^{\theta}_{\sigma},\ tu\in\mathrm{Red}^{\theta}_{\tau}\}

Lemma E.6.

(1)

If
t\in\mathrm{Red}^{\theta}_{\sigma}
, then
t\in\mathrm{SN}^{\theta}
;

(2)

if
\Gamma\vdash xu_{1}\dots u_{n}
and
u_{1},\dots,u_{n}\in\mathrm{SN}^{\theta}
, then

xu_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}^{\theta}_{\sigma}
;

(3)

If
t[u/x]v_{1}\dots v_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}^{\theta}_{\sigma}
,
and
u\in\mathrm{SN}^{\theta}
, then

(\lambda x.t)uv_{1}\dots v_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}^{\theta}_{\sigma}
;

(4)

if
tv_{1}\dots v_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}^{\theta}_{\sigma}
and

uv_{1}\dots v_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}^{\theta}_{\sigma}
, and
a\in\theta
, then

(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)v_{1}\dots v_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}^{\theta}_{\sigma}

(5)

if
tv_{1}\dots v_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}^{a\cdot\theta}_{\sigma}
and
\mathsf{FN}(v_{i})\subseteq X
, then

(\nu a.t)v_{1}\dots v_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}^{\theta}_{\sigma}
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the proof of [
[
16
]
, Lemma 28].
∎

Lemma E.7.

If
t\in\mathrm{Red}^{\theta}_{\sigma\Rightarrow\tau}
and
u\in\mathrm{Red}^{\theta}_{\sigma}
, then

\{t\}u\in\mathrm{Red}^{\theta}_{\tau}
.

Proof.

Let
\tau=\sigma_{1}\Rightarrow\dots\Rightarrow\sigma_{n}\Rightarrow o
and let
v_{1}\in\mathrm{Red}^{\theta}_{\sigma_{1}},\dots,v_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}^{\theta}_{\sigma_{n}}
.
Then
(tu)v_{1}\dots v_{n}\in\mathrm{SN}^{\theta}
which, by Lemma
E.5
, implies
\{t\}uv_{1}\dots v_{n}\in\mathrm{SN}^{\theta}
, from which we conclude
\{t\}u\in\mathrm{NRed}^{X}_{\tau}
.
∎

Proposition E.8.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t:\sigma
is derivable in
\lambda_{\to}^{\{\}}
, where
\Gamma=\{x_{1}:\sigma_{1},\dots,x_{n}:\sigma_{n}\}
, then for all
u_{i}\in\mathrm{Red}_{\sigma_{i}}^{\theta}
,

t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{Red}_{\sigma}^{\theta}
.

Proof.

The argument is by induction on
t
. All inductive cases are treated as in the proof of [
[
16
]
, Proposition 29], using the lemmas proved above; the only new case is the one below:

\bullet

if
t
is
\{t_{1}\}t_{2}
then the last rule is

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t_{1}:\sigma\Rightarrow\tau

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t_{2}:\sigma

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t:\tau

then by IH for all
u_{i}\in\mathrm{Red}_{\sigma_{i}}^{X}
,

t_{1}[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{Red}_{\sigma\Rightarrow\tau}^{\theta}
and

t_{2}[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{Red}_{\sigma}
, so by Lemma
E.7

t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]=\{t_{1}[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\}(t_{2}[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}])\in\mathrm{Red}_{\tau}^{\theta}
.

∎

Theorem E.9.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t:\sigma
is derivable in
\lambda_{\to}^{\{\}}
, then
t\in\mathrm{SN}^{\theta}
.

From this, using Proposition
E.2
we immediately deduce:

Corollary E.10.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\sigma
is derivable in
\lambda_{\to}^{\{\}}
with no 0-ary instance of the (
\vee
)-rule, then
t\in\mathrm{SN}^{\theta}
, for any suitable
\theta
.

E.1.2.
Deterministic Normalization of
\mathbf{C}\lambda_{\to,\cap}
.

We now adapt the proof of [
[
16
]
, Theorem 1] to
\mathbf{C}\lambda_{\to,\cap}
.

For this we consider an intersection type system
\lambda_{\to,\cap}
for
\Lambda_{\mathsf{{PE}}}
with the following types

\sigma::=[]\mid o\mid\mathsf{hn}\mid\mathsf{n}\mid\sigma\Rightarrow\sigma\mid\sigma\land\sigma

The rules of
\lambda_{\to,\cap}
include all those of
\lambda_{\to}^{\{\}}
except the one for
\{\}
, together with the rules in Fig.
17
.

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t:\sigma

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t:\mathsf{hn}

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t:\sigma

(
\sigma

\{[],\mathsf{hn}\}
-free)

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t:\mathsf{n}

\mathsf{FV}(t)\subseteq\Gamma

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t:[]

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t:\sigma

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t:\tau

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t:\sigma\land\tau

Figure 17
.
Typing rules for the simply typed
\lambda
-calculus
\lambda_{\to,\cap}
.

For any type
\mathfrak{s}
of
\mathbf{C}\lambda_{\to,\cap}
, we define the corresponding type
|\mathfrak{s}|
of
\lambda_{\to,\cap}
by

|\sigma|=\sigma
, for
\sigma\in\{\mathsf{hn},\mathsf{n}\}
,
|\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma|=|\mathfrak{M}|\Rightarrow|\sigma
, where

|[]|=[]
,
|[\mathfrak{s}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{s}_{n+1}]|=|\mathfrak{s}_{1}|\land\dots\land|\mathfrak{s}_{n+1}|
, and

|\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma|=|\sigma|
.

The following is easily checked by induction:

Proposition E.11.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{X}_{\lnot\vee}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
is derivable in
\mathbf{C}\lambda_{\to,\cap}
, then

|\Gamma|\vdash^{\theta}t:|\mathfrak{s}|
is derivable in
\lambda_{\to,\cap}
, for any suitable
\theta
.

Let
\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}
indicate the sets of
\lambda
-terms of
\Lambda_{\mathsf{{PE}}}
, and for all finite set of names
X
, let
\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}
indicate the
\lambda
-terms with free names in
X
.
For any finite list of names
\theta
contained in
X
, we let

\displaystyle\mathrm{HN}^{\theta}

\displaystyle:=\{t\in\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}\mid\nu^{\theta}t\text{ is head-normalizing}\}

\displaystyle\mathrm{NN}^{\theta}

\displaystyle:=\{t\in\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}\mid\nu^{\theta}t\text{ is normalizing}\}

\displaystyle\mathrm{SN}^{\theta}

\displaystyle:=\{t\in\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}\mid\nu^{\theta}t\text{ is strongly normalizing}\}

We define two families of reducibility predicates
\mathrm{NNRed}_{\sigma}^{\theta},\mathrm{HNRed}_{\sigma}^{\theta}\subseteq\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathrm{HNRed}_{[]}^{\theta}

\displaystyle:=\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}

\displaystyle\mathrm{NNRed}_{[]}^{\theta}

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{NNRed}_{\mathsf{hn}}=\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}

\displaystyle\mathrm{HNRed}_{o}^{\theta}

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{HNRed}_{\mathsf{hn}}^{\theta}=\mathrm{HNRed}_{\mathsf{hn}}^{\theta}=\mathrm{HN}^{\theta}

\displaystyle\mathrm{NNRed}_{o}^{\theta}

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{NNRed}_{\mathsf{n}}^{\theta}=\mathbb{N}^{\theta}

\displaystyle\mathrm{HNRed}_{\sigma\Rightarrow\tau}^{\theta}

\displaystyle:=\{t\in\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}\mid\forall u\in\mathrm{HNRed}_{\sigma}^{\theta},\ tu\in\mathrm{HNRed}_{\tau}^{\theta}\}

\displaystyle\mathrm{NNRed}_{\sigma\Rightarrow\tau}^{\theta}

\displaystyle:=\{t\in\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}\mid\forall u\in\mathrm{NNRed}_{\sigma}^{\theta},\ tu\in\mathrm{NNRed}_{\tau}^{\theta}\}

\displaystyle\mathrm{HNRed}_{\sigma\land\tau}^{\theta}

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{HNRed}_{\sigma}^{\theta}\cap\mathrm{HNRed}_{\tau}^{\theta}

\displaystyle\mathrm{NNRed}_{\sigma\land\tau}^{\theta}

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{NNRed}_{\sigma}^{\theta}\cap\mathrm{NNRed}_{\tau}^{\theta}

Moreover, we define a third family of reducibility predicates
\mathrm{SNRed}_{\sigma}^{\theta}\subseteq\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}
, indexed on all
\{[],\mathsf{hn}\}
-free types as follows:

\displaystyle\mathrm{SNRed}_{o}^{\theta}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{SNRed}_{\mathsf{n}}^{\theta}=\mathrm{SN}^{\theta}

\displaystyle\mathrm{SNRed}_{\sigma\Rightarrow\tau}^{\theta}

\displaystyle:=\{t\in\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}\mid\forall u\in\mathrm{NNRed}_{\sigma}^{\theta},\ tu\in\mathrm{NNRed}_{\tau}^{\theta}\}

\displaystyle\mathrm{SNRed}_{\sigma\land\tau}^{\theta}

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{NNRed}_{\sigma}^{\theta}\cap\mathrm{NNRed}_{\tau}^{\theta}

Definition E.12 (non-trivial types).

The set of
non-trivial types
is defined by induction as follows:

\bullet

o,\mathsf{hn},\mathsf{n}
are non-trivial;

\bullet

if
\tau
is non-trivial,
\sigma\Rightarrow\tau
is non-trivial;

\bullet

if either
\sigma
or
\tau
are non-trivial,
\sigma\land\tau
is non-trivial.

Lemma E.13.

For any type
\sigma
,

(i.)

if
\sigma
is non-trivial then
\mathrm{HNRed}_{\sigma}^{\theta}\subseteq\mathrm{HN}^{\theta}
;

(ii.)

if
\sigma
is
\{[],\mathsf{hn}\}
-free, then
\mathrm{NNRed}_{\sigma}^{\theta}\subseteq\mathrm{NN}^{\theta}
;

(iii.)

if
\sigma
is
\{[],\mathsf{hn}\}
-free, then
\mathrm{SNRed}_{\sigma}^{\theta}\subseteq\mathrm{SN}^{\theta}
.

Proof.

(i.) is proved in a standard way by induction on all types
\sigma
, also proving at each step
that for any term
t
of the form

xt_{1}\dots t_{n}
,
t\in\mathrm{HNRed}_{\sigma}^{\theta}
.

(ii.) is proved in a standard way by induction on all types
\sigma
, also proving at each step
that for any term
t
of the form

xt_{1}\dots t_{n}
, where
t_{1},\dots,t_{n}\in\mathrm{NN}^{\theta}
,
t\in\mathrm{NNRed}_{\sigma}^{\theta}
.

(iii.) is proved in a standard way by induction on all
\{[],\mathsf{hn}\}
-free types
\sigma
, also proving at each step
that for any term
t
of the form

xt_{1}\dots t_{n}
, where
t_{1},\dots,t_{n}\in\mathrm{SN}^{\theta}
,
t\in\mathrm{SNRed}_{\sigma}^{\theta}
.

∎

Proposition E.14.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t:\sigma
is derivable in
\lambda_{\to,\cap}
, where
\Gamma=\{x_{1}:\sigma_{1},\dots,x_{n}:\sigma_{n}\}
, and
\mathsf{FN}(t)\subseteq X
, then

(i.)

for all
u_{i}\in\mathrm{HNRed}_{\sigma_{i}}^{\theta}
,

t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{HNRed}_{\sigma}
;

(ii.)

for all
u_{i}\in\mathrm{NNRed}_{\sigma_{i}}^{\theta}
,

t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{NNRed}_{\sigma}
;

(iii.)

if the types
[]
and
\mathsf{hn}
never occurs in the derivation, then
for all
u_{i}\in\mathrm{SNRed}_{\sigma_{i}}^{\theta}
,

t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{SNRed}_{\sigma}
;

Proof.

Both arguments are by induction on the typing derivation of
t
. All inductive cases are treated as in the proof of [
[
16
]
, Proposition 29], except for those below (we consider here only case (ii.)):

\bullet

if the last rule is

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t:\sigma

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t:\mathsf{hn}

then for all
u_{i}\in\mathrm{NNRed}_{\sigma_{i}}^{\theta}
,

t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}=\mathrm{NNRed}_{\mathsf{hn}}^{\theta}
.

\bullet

if the last rule is

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t:\sigma

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t:\mathsf{n}

where
\sigma
is
\{[],\mathsf{hn}\}
-free,
then for all
u_{i}\in\mathrm{NNRed}_{\sigma_{i}}^{\theta}
,

t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{NNRed}_{\sigma}^{\theta}
, and by Lemma
E.13

\mathrm{NNRed}_{\sigma}^{\theta}\subseteq\mathrm{NN}^{\theta}=\mathrm{NNRed}_{\mathsf{n}}^{\theta}
, so

t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{NNRed}_{\mathsf{n}}^{\theta}
.

\bullet

if the last rule is

\mathsf{FN}(t)\subseteq X

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t:[]

then for all
u_{i}\in\mathrm{NNRed}_{\sigma_{i}}^{\theta}
,

t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}=\mathrm{NNRed}_{[]}^{\theta}
.

\bullet

if the last rule is

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t:\sigma

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t:\tau

\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t:\sigma\land\tau

then for all
u_{i}\in\mathrm{NNRed}_{\sigma_{i}}^{\theta}
, by IH

t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{NNRed}_{\sigma}^{\theta}
and

t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{NNRed}_{\tau}^{\theta}
, whence

t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{NNRed}_{\sigma}^{\theta}\cap\mathrm{NNRed}_{\tau}^{\theta}=\mathrm{NNRed}_{\sigma\land\tau}^{\theta}
.

∎

Proposition E.15.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{\theta}t:\sigma
is derivable in
\lambda_{\to,\cap}
, then:

(i.)

if
[]
is non-trivial, then
\nu^{\theta}t
is head-normalizable;

(ii.)

if
\Gamma
and
\sigma
are
\{[],\mathsf{hn}\}
-free, then
\nu^{\theta}t
is normalizable;

(iii.)

if
[]
and
\mathsf{hn}
never occur in the derivation, then
\nu^{\theta}t
is strongly normalizable.

Using Proposition
E.11

Corollary E.16.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{X}_{\lnot\vee}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
in
\mathbf{C}\lambda_{\to,\cap}
, then for all suitable
\theta
:

(i.)

\nu^{\theta}t
is head-normalizable;

(ii.)

if
\Gamma
and
\sigma
are
\{[],\mathsf{hn}\}
-free, then
\nu^{\theta}t
is normalizable;

(iii.)

if
[]
and
\mathsf{hn}
never occur in the derivation, then
\nu^{\theta}t
is strongly normalizable.

E.2.
Probabilistic Reducibility Candidates for
\Lambda_{\mathsf{{PE}}}
.

In this subsection and the following one we work with a general class of types which comprises both those of
\mathbf{C}\lambda^{\{\}}_{\to}
and those of
\mathbf{C}\lambda_{\to,\cap}
.
These types are defined by the grammar below:

\sigma::=[]\mid o\mid\mathsf{hn}\mid\mathsf{n}\mid\sigma\Rightarrow\sigma\mid\sigma\land\sigma\mid\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma

It is clear that any type of
\mathbf{C}\lambda^{\{\}}_{\to}
is a type of the grammar above. For the types of
\mathbf{C}\lambda_{\to,\cap}
it suffices to see

{M}=[\mathfrak{s}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{s}_{n}]
as
(\dots(\mathfrak{s}_{1}\land\mathfrak{s}_{2})\land\dots\land\mathfrak{s}_{n-1})\land\mathfrak{s}_{n}
, where
n>0
, and as
[]
otherwise.

For any type
\sigma
, we let the positive real
\lceil\sigma\rceil\in[0,1]
be defined by
\lceil[]\rceil=0
,
\lceil o\rceil=\lceil\mathsf{hn}\rceil=\lceil\mathsf{n}\rceil=1
,
\lceil\sigma\Rightarrow\tau\rceil=\lceil\sigma\rceil\cdot\lceil\tau\rceil
,
\lceil\sigma\land\tau\rceil=\max\{\lceil\sigma\rceil,\lceil\tau\rceil\}
, and
\lceil\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma\rceil=q\cdot\lceil\sigma\rceil
.

One can check that for all type
\sigma
coming from either
\mathbf{C}\lambda^{\{\}}_{\to}
or
\mathbf{C}\lambda_{\to,\cap}
,
\lceil\sigma\rceil>0
.

Let us establish a few preliminary lemmas.

Lemma E.17.

For all
t\in{T}
and variables
x,y
, with
y
not occurring in
t
,

(i.)

\mathrm{HNV}(t)=\mathrm{HNV}(t[y/x])
;

(ii.)

\mathrm{NF}(t)=\mathrm{NF}(t[y/x])
.

Proof.

We only prove case (ii.), the other one being proved in a similar way.
Observe that if
t
is not a strong head normal form and
{D}_{t}(u)>0
, it must be that
u
is of size strictly smaller that
t
. So we can argue by induction on
t
:

\bullet

if
t=\lambda\vec{x}.zt_{1}\dots t_{n}
, then
\mathrm{NF}(t)=\prod_{i}\mathrm{NF}(t_{i})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\small[\text{I.H.}]}}{{=}}\prod_{i}\mathrm{NF}(t_{i}[y/x])=\mathrm{NF}(t[y/x])
;

\bullet

otherwise, since
{D}_{t}(u)={D}_{t[y/x]}(u[y/x])
, we have that
\mathrm{NF}(t)=\sum_{u\in\mathrm{HNV}}{D}_{t}(u)\cdot\mathrm{NF}(u)=\sum_{u\in\mathrm{HNV}}{D}_{t[y/x]}(u[y/x])\cdot\mathrm{NF}(u)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\small[\text{I.H.}]}}{{=}}\sum_{u\in\mathrm{HNV}}{D}_{t[y/x]}(u[y/x])\cdot\mathrm{NF}(u[y/x])=\sum_{u\in\mathrm{HNV}}{D}_{t[y/x]}(u)\cdot\mathrm{NF}(u)=\mathrm{NF}(t[y/x])
.

∎

Lemma E.18.

For any name-closed term
t
,

(i.)

\mathrm{HNV}(t)=\mathrm{HNV}(\lambda y.t)
;

(ii.)

\mathrm{NF}(t)=\mathrm{NF}(\lambda y.t)
.

Proof.

We only prove case (ii.), the other one being proved in a similar way.
We argue by induction on
t
:

\bullet

if
t\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}\{\}}^{*}\lambda\vec{x}.zt_{1}\dots t_{n}
, then
\lambda y.t\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}\{\}}^{*}\lambda y\lambda\vec{x}.zt_{1}\dots t_{n}
, so by definition
\mathrm{NF}(t)=\prod_{i}\mathrm{NF}(t_{i})=\mathrm{NF}(\lambda y.t)
;

\bullet

otherwise, if
t\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}\{\}}^{*}\nu a.t^{\prime}
, then
\lambda y.t\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}\{\}}^{*}\nu a.t^{\lambda y}
, and one can show by induction on a
\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}
-reduction path that

{D}_{t^{\prime}}(u)={D}_{t^{\lambda y}}(\lambda y.u)
. So we have that

\mathrm{NF}(t)=\sum_{u\in\mathrm{HNV}}{D}_{t^{\prime}}(u)\cdot\mathrm{NF}(u)=\sum_{u\in\mathrm{HNV}}{D}_{t^{\lambda y}}(\lambda y.u)\cdot\mathrm{NF}(u)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\small\text{[I.H.]}}}{{=}}\sum_{u\in\mathrm{HNV}}{D}_{t^{\lambda y}}(\lambda y.u)\cdot\mathrm{NF}(\lambda y.u)=\sum_{u\in\mathrm{HNV}}{D}_{t^{\lambda y}}(u)\cdot\mathrm{NF}(u)=\mathrm{NF}(\lambda y.t)
.

∎

Lemma E.19.

For any name-closed term
t
,

(i.)

\mathrm{HNV}(t)\geq\mathrm{HNV}(tx)
;

(ii.)

\mathrm{NF}(t)\geq\mathrm{NF}(tx)
.

Proof.

We only prove case (ii.), the other one being proved in a similar way.
Suppose
tx\rightarrowtriangle^{*}u
and
\mathrm{NF}(u)\geq q
. We will show that
\mathrm{NF}(t)\geq q
. Two cases arise:

\bullet

u=u^{\prime}x
where
t\rightarrowtriangle^{*}u^{\prime}
. Then
u^{\prime}
cannot start with either
\nu
(otherwise it would not be a PNF, since
(\nu a.v)x\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}\{\}}\nu a.vx
),
\oplus
(since
(v_{1}\oplus_{a}^{i}v_{2})x\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}\{\}}(v_{1}x)\oplus_{a}^{i}(v_{2}x)
), or
\lambda
(since
(\lambda y.v)x
is a
\beta
-redex, which would imply
\mathrm{NF}(u)=0
). Hence
u^{\prime}
is of the form

yu_{1}\dots u_{n}
, and thus
\mathrm{NF}(u)=\mathrm{NF}(u^{\prime})\geq q
, which implies
\mathrm{NF}(t)\geq q
.

\bullet

tx\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}}^{*}(\lambda y.t^{\prime})x
, and
t^{\prime}[y\mapsto x]\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}}^{*}u
. Then

t
reduces to the PNF
\lambda y.u[x\mapsto y]
, and we can conclude by Lemma
E.17
and Lemma
E.18
.

∎

In the following, we will sometimes write
t\in\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{r}
(resp.
t\in\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{r}
) for
\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}(t)\geq r
(resp.
\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}(t)\geq r
).

We will define two different classes of
reducibility predicates
, one related to probabilistic head normal forms and the other related to probabilistic normal forms.
For any type
\sigma
, finite set
X
of names,
r\in[0,1]
, and
S\subseteq(2^{\mathbb{N}})^{X}
, we define the sets

\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S),\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)\subseteq\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}
by induction as follows:

\displaystyle\mathrm{HRed}_{[]}^{X,r}(S)

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}

\displaystyle\mathrm{HRed}_{o}^{X,r}(S)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{HRed}_{\mathsf{hn}}^{X,r}(S)=\mathrm{HRed}_{\mathsf{n}}^{X,r}(S)=\{t\in\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}\mid\forall\omega\in S\ \pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\in\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{r}\}

\displaystyle\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma\Rightarrow\tau}^{X,r}(S)

\displaystyle=\{t\in\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}\mid\forall S^{\prime}\subseteq S,\forall s\in(0,1],\ \forall u\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{X,1}(S^{\prime}),\ tu\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\tau}^{X,r}(S^{\prime})\}

\displaystyle\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma\land\tau}^{X,r}(S)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)\cap\mathrm{HRed}_{\tau}^{X,r}(S)

\displaystyle\mathrm{HRed}_{\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma}^{X,r}(S)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{X,qr}(S)

\displaystyle\mathrm{NRed}_{[]}^{X,r}(S)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathsf{hn}}^{X,r}(S)=\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}

\displaystyle\mathrm{NRed}_{o}^{X,r}(S)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathsf{n}}^{X,r}(S)=\{t\in\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}\mid\forall\omega\in S\ \pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\in\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{r}\}

\displaystyle\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma\Rightarrow\tau}^{X,r}(S)

\displaystyle=\{t\in\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}\mid\forall S^{\prime}\subseteq S,\forall s\in(0,1],\ \forall u\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,1}(S^{\prime}),\ tu\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,r}(S^{\prime})\}

\displaystyle\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma\land\tau}^{X,r}(S)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)\cap\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,r}(S)

\displaystyle\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma}^{X,r}(S)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,qr}(S)

We will now establish a few properties of the families
\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
and
\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
. Since the proofs for the two cases are similar, we will provide details only for the second case.

Lemma E.20.

For any type
\sigma
and
q,r\in[0,1]
, if
q\geq r
, then

(i.)

\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{q}(S)\subseteq\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{r}(S)
.

(ii.)

\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{q}(S)\subseteq\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{r}(S)
.

Proof.

We only prove case (ii.), the other one being proved in a similar way.
By induction on
\sigma
:

\bullet

if
\sigma\in\{[],\mathsf{hn}\}
, the claim is immediate;

\bullet

if
\sigma\in\{o,,\mathsf{n}\}
, then
t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{q}(S)
iff for all
\omega\in S
,
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\in\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{q}\subseteq\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{r}
, so

t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{r}(S)
;

\bullet

if
\sigma=(\tau\Rightarrow\rho)
and
t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,q}(S)
then for all
S^{\prime}\subseteq S
,
u\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,1}(S^{\prime})
,

tu\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\rho}^{q}(S^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\small\text{[I.H.]}}}{{\subseteq}}\mathrm{NRed}_{\rho}^{r}(S^{\prime})

; hence we can conclude
t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{r}(S)
;

\bullet

if
\sigma=\tau\land\rho
and
t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,q}(S)
, then by IH
t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,r}(S)
and
t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S)
