, so
t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
;

\bullet

if
\sigma=\mathbf{C}^{s}\tau
, then

\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,q}(S)=\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,sq}(S)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\small\text{[I.H.]}}}{{\subseteq}}\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,sr}(S)=\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
.

∎

Lemma E.21.

(i.)

\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(\emptyset)=\{t\in\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}\mid\mathsf{FN}(t)\subseteq X\}
;

(ii.)

\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(\emptyset)=\{t\in\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}\mid\mathsf{FN}(t)\subseteq X\}
.

Proof.

We only prove (ii.) as (i.) is proved similarly.
Let
\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}=\{t\in\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}\mid\mathsf{FN}(t)\subseteq X\}
.
Observe that the inclusion
\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(\emptyset)\subseteq\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}
is immediate. For the converse direction, we argue by induction on
\sigma
:

\bullet

if
\sigma\in\{[],\mathsf{hn}\}
, the claim is immediate;

\bullet

If
\sigma\in\{o,\mathsf{n}\}
, then trivially for all
\omega\in\emptyset
and
t\in\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}
,
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\in\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{r}
, so
\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}\subseteq\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(\emptyset)
.

\bullet

if
\sigma=\tau\Rightarrow\rho
, then for all
t\in\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}
, and
u\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,1}(\emptyset)
,
tu\in\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}
, so by IH
tu\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\rho}^{X,r}(\emptyset)
.
We can thus conclude that
t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(\emptyset)
.

\bullet

if
\sigma=\tau\land\rho
, then for all
t\in\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}
, by IH
t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,r}(\emptyset)
and
t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\rho}^{X,r}(\emptyset)
, so
t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(\emptyset)
.

\bullet

If
\sigma=\mathbf{C}^{q}\tau
, then by the I.H.
\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}\subseteq\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,qr}(\emptyset)=\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,q}(\emptyset)
.

∎

Lemma E.22.

(i.)

t\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
iff for all
\omega\in S
,
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{\emptyset,r}
;

(ii.)

t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
iff for all
\omega\in S
,
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{\emptyset,r}
.

Proof.

We only prove (ii.) as (i.) is proved similarly.
We argue by induction on
\sigma
:

\bullet

if
\sigma\in\{[],\mathsf{hn}\}
, the claim is immediate;

\bullet

If
\sigma\in\{o,\mathsf{n}\}
, the claim follows from the definition.

\bullet

If
\sigma=\tau\Rightarrow\rho
, suppose
t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
, let
\omega\in S
and
u\in\mathrm{NRed}^{\emptyset,1}_{\tau}
. Then
u
is name-closed hence for all
g\in S
,
\pi^{g}(u)=u
, which by the IH, implies
u\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,1}(S)
; we deduce then that
tu\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S)
, so by IH
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(tu)=\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)u\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\rho}^{\emptyset,r}
. By a similar argument we can show that for all
v\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{\emptyset,s}
,
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(\{t\}v)=\{\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\}v\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\rho}^{\emptyset,rs}
.
We can thus conclude that
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{\emptyset,r}
.

Conversely, suppose that for all
\omega\in S
,
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{\emptyset,r}
, let
S^{\prime}\subseteq S
and
u\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,1}(S^{\prime})
; if
\omega\in S^{\prime}
, then by IH
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(u)\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{\emptyset,1}
, so
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(tu)=\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\pi^{\omega}_{X}(u)\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\rho}^{\emptyset,r}
;
We have thus proved that for all
\omega\in S^{\prime}
,
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(tu)\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\rho}^{\emptyset,r}

, which by IH implies that
tu\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S^{\prime})

We conclude then that
t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
.

\bullet

if
\sigma=\tau\land\rho
then
t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
iff
t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,r}(S)
and
t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S)
iff (by the IH) for all
\omega\in S
,
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,r}(\emptyset)
and
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\rho}^{X,r}(\emptyset)
, iff for all
\omega\in S
,
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(\emptyset)
.

\bullet

If
\sigma=\mathbf{C}^{q}\tau
, then

t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)\text{ iff }t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,rq}(S)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{[I.H.]}}}{{\text{ iff }}}\forall\omega\in S\ \pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,rq}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{[I.H.]}}}{{\text{ iff }}}\forall\omega\in S\ \pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}
.

∎

Lemma E.23.

(i.)

If
t\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
and
t^{\prime}\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}t
, then
t^{\prime}\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
.

(ii.)

If
t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
and
t^{\prime}\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}}t
, then
t^{\prime}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
.

Proof.

By induction on
\sigma
.
∎

Lemma E.24.

For all terms
t,u_{1},\dots,u_{n}
with
\mathsf{FN}(t)\subseteq X\cup\{a\}
,
\mathsf{FN}(u_{1}),\dots,\mathsf{FN}(u_{n})\subseteq X
, and measurable sets
S_{1},\dots,S_{k+1}\subseteq(2^{\mathbb{N}})^{X\cup\{a\}}
and
S^{\prime}\subseteq(2^{\mathbb{N}})^{X}
, if

(1)

the
S_{i}
are pairwise disjoint;

(2)

for all
\omega\in S_{i}
,
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(tu_{1}\dots u_{n})\in\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{r_{i}}
(resp.
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(tu_{1}\dots u_{n})\in\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{r_{i}}
);

(3)

for all
\omega\in S^{\prime}
,

\mu(\Pi^{\omega}(S_{i}))\geq s_{i}
;

then for all
\omega\in S^{\prime}
,
\pi^{\omega}_{X}((\nu a.t)u_{1}\dots u_{n})\in\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}r_{i}s_{i}}

(resp.
\pi^{\omega}_{X}((\nu a.t)u_{1}\dots u_{n})\in\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}r_{i}s_{i}}
).

Proof.

We only prove the claim in the case of
\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}
, the other case being proved similarly.
Let us first consider the case where
n=0
.
Let
\omega\in S^{\prime}
. Since any term reduces to a (unique) PNF, we can suppose w.l.o.g. that the name-closed term
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(\nu a.t)=\nu a.t^{*}
is in PNF. Then by Corollary
B.5

t^{*}
is a
(\mathcal{T},a)
-tree of level
I_{a}(t^{*})
.
Observe that for all
\omega^{\prime}\in(2^{\mathbb{N}})^{\{a\}}
,

\pi^{\omega^{\prime}}_{\{a\}}(t^{*})=\pi^{\omega^{\prime}}_{\{a\}}(\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t))=\pi^{\omega+\omega^{\prime}}_{X\cup\{a\}}(t)
.

If
N
is the cardinality of
\mathrm{Supp}(t^{*})
, by 3. and the fact that
s_{i}>0
we deduce that for all
i=1,\dots,k+1
there exists a finite number
K_{i}
of elements
w_{i1},\dots,w_{iK_{i}}
of
\mathrm{Supp}(t^{*})
such that

\pi^{\omega_{ij}}_{\{a\}}(t^{*})=w_{ij}\in\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{r_{i}}
for some
\omega_{ij}\in(2^{\mathbb{N}})^{\{a\}}
such that
\omega+\omega_{ij}\in S_{i}
, and using 3. we deduce that

\sum_{w\in\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{r_{i}}}\mu\{\omega\mid\pi^{\omega}_{\{a\}}(t^{*})=w\}\geq\sum_{{j}=1}^{K_{i}}\mu\{\omega\mid\pi^{\omega}_{\{a\}}(t^{*})=w_{ij}\}\geq s_{i}

Now, by reducing, for all
i\leq k+1
, each such term
w_{ij}
inside
\nu a.t
, to some PNF
w^{\prime}_{ij}\in\mathrm{NF}^{r_{i}}
, we obtain a new PNF
\nu a.t^{\sharp}
and we can compute (using the fact that
\sum_{u\in\mathrm{HNV}}{D}_{w^{\prime}_{ij}}(u)\cdot\mathrm{NF}(u)\geq r_{i}
):

\displaystyle\mathrm{NF}(\nu a.t^{\sharp})=\sum_{u\in\mathrm{HNV}}\mathcal{D}_{\nu a.t^{\sharp}}(u)\cdot\mathrm{NF}(u)

\displaystyle=\sum_{u\in\mathrm{HNV}}\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Supp}(t^{\sharp})}\mathcal{D}_{t^{\prime}}(u)\cdot\mu\{\omega\mid\pi^{\omega}_{\{a\}}(t^{\sharp})=t^{\prime}\}\right)\cdot\mathrm{NF}(u)

\displaystyle=\sum_{u\in\mathrm{HNV}}\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Supp}(t^{\sharp})}\mathcal{D}_{t^{\prime}}(u)\cdot\mathrm{NF}(u)\cdot\mu\{\omega\mid\pi^{\omega}_{\{a\}}(t^{\sharp})=t^{\prime}\}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Supp}(t^{\sharp})}\left(\sum_{u\in\mathrm{HNV}}\mathcal{D}_{t^{\prime}}(u)\cdot\mathrm{NF}(u)\cdot\mu\{\omega\mid\pi^{\omega}_{\{a\}}(t^{\sharp})=t^{\prime}\}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Supp}(t^{\sharp})}\left(\sum_{u\in\mathrm{HNV}}\mathcal{D}_{t^{\prime}}(u)\cdot\mathrm{NF}(u)\right)\cdot\mu\{\omega\mid\pi^{\omega}_{\{a\}}(t^{\sharp})=t^{\prime}\}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}\sum_{{j}=1}^{K_{i}}\left(\sum_{u\in\mathrm{HNV}}\mathcal{D}_{w^{\prime}_{ij}}(u)\cdot\mathrm{NF}(u)\right)\cdot\mu\{\omega\mid\pi^{\omega}_{\{a\}}(t^{\sharp})=w^{\prime}_{ij}\}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}\sum_{{j}=1}^{K_{i}}r_{i}\cdot\mu\{\omega\mid\pi^{\omega}_{\{a\}}(t^{\sharp})=w^{\prime}_{ij}\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}r_{i}\cdot\left(\sum_{{j}=1}^{K_{i}}\mu\{\omega\mid\pi^{\omega}_{\{a\}}(t^{\sharp})=w^{\prime}_{ij}\}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}r_{i}\cdot\left(\sum_{{j}=1}^{K_{i}}\mu\{\omega\mid\pi^{\omega}_{\{a\}}(t^{*})=w^{\prime}_{ij}\}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}r_{i}\cdot s_{i}

Now, from
\nu a.t\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}}^{*}\nu a.t^{\sharp}\in\mathrm{NF}^{\sum_{i}r_{i}s_{i}}
, we conclude
\nu a.t\in\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{\sum_{i}r_{i}s_{i}}
.

For the case in which
n>0
, we argue as follows:
from the hypotheses we deduce by the first point that

\nu a.(tu_{1}u_{2}\dots u_{n})\in\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{\sum_{i}r_{i}s_{i}}
. We can then conclude by observing that

(\nu a.t)u_{1}u_{2}\dots u_{n}\rightarrowtriangle_{\mathsf{p}\{\}}\nu a.(tu_{1}u_{2}\dots u_{n})
.
∎

Lemma E.25.

For all types
\sigma
, terms
t,u_{1},\dots,u_{n}
with
\mathsf{FN}(t)\subseteq X\cup\{a\}
,
\mathsf{FN}(u_{i})\subseteq X
,
and measurable sets
S_{1},\dots,S_{k+1}\subseteq(2^{\mathbb{N}})^{X\cup\{a\}}
and
S^{\prime}\subseteq(2^{\mathbb{N}})^{X}
, if

(1)

the
S_{i}
are pairwise disjoint;

(2)

tu_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{X\cup\{a\},r_{i}}(S_{i})
(resp.
tu_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X\cup\{a\},r_{i}}(S_{i})
), for all
i=1,\dots,k+1
;

(3)

for all
\omega\in S^{\prime}
,
\mu(\Pi^{\omega}(S_{i}))\geq s_{i}
;

then
(\nu a.t)u_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{X,\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}r_{i}\cdot s_{i}}(S^{\prime})

(resp.
(\nu a.t)u_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}r_{i}\cdot s_{i}}(S^{\prime})
).

Proof.

We only prove the claim for
\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}
, the other case being proved similarly. We argue by induction on
\sigma
:

\bullet

if
\sigma\in\{[],\mathsf{hn}\}
, the claim is immediate;

\bullet

if
\sigma\in\{o,\mathsf{n}\}
, then the claim follows from Lemma
E.24
.

\bullet

if
\sigma=\tau\Rightarrow\rho
, then let
S^{\prime\prime}\subseteq S^{\prime}
and
u\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,1}(S^{\prime\prime})
.
For all
i
, since
s_{i}>0
for all
\omega\in S^{\prime\prime}\subseteq S^{\prime}
there exists at least one
\omega^{\prime}\in(2^{\mathbb{N}})^{\{a\}}
, such that
\omega+\omega^{\prime}\in S_{i}
.
Let
T_{i}=\{\omega+\omega^{\prime}\in S_{i}\mid\omega\in S^{\prime\prime}\}
. The
T_{i}
are pairwise disjoint and measurable, since they are counter-images of a measurable set through a measurable function (the projection function from
(2^{\mathbb{N}})^{X\cup\{a\}}
to
(2^{\mathbb{N}})^{X}
).

We have that
u\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,1}(T_{i})
, for all
i=1,\dots,k+1
: for all
\omega+\omega^{\prime}\in S^{\prime\prime}
,
\pi^{\omega+\omega^{\prime}}_{X\cup\{a\}}(u)=\pi^{\omega}_{X}(u)\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{\emptyset,1}
, so by Lemma
E.22
,
u\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,1}(T_{i})
.
Since
T_{i}\subseteq S_{i}
, using the hypothesis 2. we deduce that
tu_{1}\dots u_{n}u\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\rho}^{X,r_{i}}(T_{i})
; moreover, for all
\omega\in S^{\prime\prime}
,
\mu(\Pi^{\omega}(T_{i}))=\mu(\Pi^{\omega}(S_{i}))\geq s_{i}
. So, by the induction hypothesis
(\nu a.t)u_{1}\dots u_{n}u\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\rho}^{X,\sum_{i}r_{i}s_{i}}(S^{\prime\prime})=\mathrm{NRed}_{\rho}^{X,\sum_{i}r_{i}s_{i}}(S^{\prime\prime})
.
We can thus conclude that
(\nu a.t)u_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,\sum_{i}r_{i}s_{i}}(S^{\prime})
.

\bullet

\sigma=\tau\land\rho
, then from the hypotheses by IH we deduce
(\nu a.t)u_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,\sum r_{i}s_{i}}(S^{\prime})

and
(\nu a.t)u_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\rho}^{X,\sum r_{i}s_{i}}(S^{\prime})
, and thus

(\nu a.t)u_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,\sum r_{i}s_{i}}(S^{\prime})
.

\bullet

if
\sigma=\mathbf{C}^{q}\tau
, the claim follows from the induction hypothesis.

∎

Lemma E.26.

(i.)

t[u/x]u_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)

\Rightarrow

(\lambda x.t)uu_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
.

(ii.)

t[u/x]u_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)

\Rightarrow

(\lambda x.t)uu_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
.

Proof.

We only prove claim (ii.) as claim (i.) is proved similarly.
By induction on
\sigma
:

\bullet

if
\sigma\in\{[],\mathsf{hn}\}
, the claim is immediate;

\bullet

If
\sigma\in\{o,\mathsf{n}\}
, then if
t[u/x]u_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
, then for all
\omega\in S
,

\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t[u/x]u_{1}\dots u_{n})=\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)[\pi^{\omega}_{X}(u)/x]\pi^{\omega}_{X}(u_{1})\dots\pi^{\omega}_{X}(u_{n})\in\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{r}
. Since the reduction tree of
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t^{\prime\prime})
, where

t^{\prime\prime}=(\lambda x.t)uu_{1}\dots u_{n}
, is contained in that of
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t[u/x]u_{1}\dots u_{n})
, we deduce
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t^{\prime\prime})\in\mathrm{NF}^{r}
, and we conclude that

t^{\prime\prime}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
.

\bullet

If
\sigma=\tau\Rightarrow\rho
, then let
S^{\prime}\subseteq S
and
v\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,1}(S^{\prime})
; then
t[u/x]u_{1}\dots u_{n}v\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S^{\prime})
so by IH
(\lambda x.t)uu_{1}\dots u_{n}v\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S^{\prime})
. This proves in particular that
(\lambda x.t)uu_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
.

\bullet

If
\sigma=\tau\land\rho
and
t[u/x]u_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
, then

t[u/x]u_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,r}(S)
and
t[u/x]u_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,r}(S)
, so by IH

(\lambda x.t)uu_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,r}(S)
and
(\lambda x.t)uu_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S)
,
which implies
(\lambda x.t)uu_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
.

\bullet

If
\sigma=\mathbf{C}^{q}\tau
, then if
t[u/x]u_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)=\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,rq}(S)
, then by I.H.

(\lambda x.t)uu_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,rq}(S)=\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
.

∎

Lemma E.27.

For all
t
such that
\mathsf{FN}(t)\subseteq X
and measurable sets
S,S^{\prime}\subseteq(2^{\mathbb{N}})^{X}
,

(i.)

If for all
S^{\prime\prime}\subseteq S^{\prime}
and

u\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{X,s}(S^{\prime\prime})
,
t[u/x]\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\tau}^{X,rs}(S\cap S^{\prime\prime})
, then
\lambda x.t\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma\Rightarrow\tau}^{X,r}(S\cap S^{\prime})
.

(ii.)

If for all
S^{\prime\prime}\subseteq S^{\prime}
and

u\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,s}(S^{\prime\prime})
,
t[u/x]\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,rs}(S\cap S^{\prime\prime})
, then
\lambda x.t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma\Rightarrow\tau}^{X,r}(S\cap S^{\prime})
.

Proof.

As usual, we only prove claim (ii.).
Let
T\subseteq S\cap S^{\prime}
and
u\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,s}(T)
.
Then, by hypothesis,
t[u/x]\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,rs}(S\cap T)=\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,rs}(S\cap S^{\prime})
and we conclude by Lemma
E.26
that
(\lambda x.t)u\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,rs}(S\cap S^{\prime})
.
This proves that
\lambda x.t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma\Rightarrow\tau}^{X,r}(S\cap S^{\prime})
.

∎

Lemma E.28.

For any two types
\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{t}
of
\mathbf{C}\lambda_{\to,\cap}
, if
\mathfrak{s}\preceq\mathfrak{t}
, then for all
S\subseteq(2^{\mathbb{N}})^{X}
,

(i.)

\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)\subseteq\mathrm{HRed}_{\tau}^{X,r}(S)
;

(ii.)

\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)\subseteq\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,r}(S)
.

Proof.

As usual, we only prove claim (ii.).
We argue by induction on
\mathfrak{s}
:

\bullet

if
\mathfrak{s}\in\{[],o,\mathsf{hn},\mathsf{n}\}
, then it must be
\mathfrak{s}=\mathfrak{t}
, so the claim is immediate.

\bullet

If
\mathfrak{s}=[\mathfrak{M}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{M}_{n}]\Rightarrow\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}
, then

\mathfrak{t}=[\mathfrak{N}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{N}_{m}]\Rightarrow\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}
, where
\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}\preceq\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}
, and
\mathfrak{s}_{i}\succeq\mathfrak{t}_{f(i)}
holds for some injective function
f:\{1,\dots,n\}\to\{1,\dots,m\}
.
By the I.H. we have then that
\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}}^{X,r}(S)\subseteq\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}}^{X,r}(S)
and

\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{t}_{f(i)}}^{X,1}(S^{\prime})\subseteq\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{s}_{i}}^{X,1}(S^{\prime})
; by the definition of
\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{s}}^{X,r}(S)
this implies then
\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{s}}^{X,r}(S)\subseteq\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{t}}^{X,r}(S)
.

\bullet

if
\mathfrak{s}=\mathbf{C}^{q}\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}
, then it must be
\mathfrak{t}=\mathbf{C}^{s}\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}
, with
\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}\preceq\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}
and
q\geq s
; then by IH and Lemma
E.20
we have

\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{s}}^{X,r}(S)=\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}}^{X,qr}(S)\subseteq\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}}^{X,sr}(S)\subseteq\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}}^{X,sr}(S)=\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{t}}^{X,r}(S)
.

∎

We now show how the two families of reducibility candidates differ on the kind of normalization properties they warrant.

Let us introduce a (first) notion of
neutral term
for
\Lambda_{\mathsf{{PE}}}
:

Definition E.29 (neutral terms, version 1).

For any
measurable set
S\subseteq(2^{\mathbb{N}})^{X}
, the set
\mathrm{Neut}(S)
is defined by induction as follows:

\bullet

for any variable
x
,
x\in\mathrm{Neut}(S)
;

\bullet

if
t\in\mathrm{Neut}(S)
then for all
u\in\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}
,
tu\in\mathrm{Neut}(S)
.

It is easily checked that for all
t\in\mathrm{Neut}(S)
and
\omega\in S
,
\mathrm{HNV}(\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t))=1
.

Probabilistic neutral terms can be used to show that reducible terms reduce to a probabilistic head normal form.

Definition E.30 (non-trivial type).

The set of
non-trivial types
is defined by induction as follows:

\bullet

o,\mathsf{hn},\mathsf{n}
are non-trivial;

\bullet

if
\tau
is non-trivial,
\sigma\Rightarrow\tau
is non-trivial;

\bullet

if either
\sigma
or
\tau
are non-trivial,
\sigma\land\tau
is non-trivial;

\bullet

if
\sigma
is non-trivial,
\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma
is non-trivial.

Lemma E.31.

For any type
\sigma
:

(1)

if
\sigma
is non-trivial and

t\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
and
\omega\in S
, then
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\in\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{\lceil\sigma\rceil\cdot r}
;

(2)

\mathrm{Neut}(S)\subseteq\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{X,1}(S)
.

Proof.

We argue by induction on
\sigma
:

\bullet

if
\sigma=[]
, then both claims are immediate;

\bullet

if
\sigma\in\{o,\mathsf{hn},\mathsf{n}\}
then by definition
t\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
iff for any
\omega\in S
,
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\in\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{r}=\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{\lceil\sigma\rceil r}
.
Moreover, if
t\in\mathrm{Neut}(S)
, then
\mathrm{HNV}(t)=1
, so
t\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{X,1}(S)
.

\bullet

if
\sigma=\tau\Rightarrow\rho
and
t\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
, then by IH
x\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\tau}^{X,1}(S)
, hence

tx\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\tau}^{X,r}(S)
, and again by IH for all
\omega\in S

\pi^{\omega}_{X}(tx)=\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)x\in\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{\lceil\tau\rceil\cdot r}=\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{\lceil\sigma\rceil\cdot r}
, and thus
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\in\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{\lceil\sigma\rceil\cdot r}
.

Moreover, if
t\in\mathrm{Neut}(S)
, let
S^{\prime}\subseteq S
and
u\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\tau}^{X,1}(S)
, then by IH for all
\omega\in S^{\prime}
,
\pi^{\omega}(u)\in\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{\lceil\tau\rceil}
and thus
tu\in\mathrm{Neut}(S^{\prime})
, so by IH
tu\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\rho}^{X,1}(S^{\prime})\subseteq\mathrm{HRed}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S^{\prime})
; we can thus conclude that
t\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{X,1}(S)
.

\bullet

if
\sigma=\tau\land\rho
and
t\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
, then
t\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\tau}^{X,r}(S)
and
t\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S)
, so by IH for all
\omega\in S
,
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\in\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{\lceil\tau\rceil r}
and
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\in\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{\lceil\rho\rceil r}
; hence we deduce that for all
\omega\in S

\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\in\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{\max\{\lceil\tau\rceil,\lceil\rho\rceil\}\cdot r}=\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{\lceil\sigma\rceil\cdot r}
.

Moreover, if
t\in\mathrm{Neut}(S)
, then by IH
t\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\tau}^{X,1}(S)
and
t\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\rho}^{X,1}(S)
, so
t\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{X,1}(S)
.

\bullet

if
\sigma=\mathbf{C}^{s}\tau
and
t\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{X,q}(S)=\mathrm{HRed}_{\tau}^{X,qs}(S)
, then by IH
for all
\omega\in S
,
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\in\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{\lceil\tau\rceil qs}=\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{\lceil\sigma\rceil q}
.
Moreover, if
t\in\mathrm{Neut}(S)
, then by IH
t\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\tau}^{X,1}(S)\subseteq\mathrm{HRed}_{\tau}^{X,s}(S)=\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{X,1}(S)
.

∎

To establish a result like Lemma
E.31
for the family
\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
, as discussed in Section
6
, we need to restrict to
balanced
types. For our general class of types, these are defined as follows:

Definition E.32.

The set
\mathrm{Bal}
of
balanced types
is defined inductively as follows:

\bullet

\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}\sigma
, for
\sigma\in\{o,\mathsf{hn},\mathsf{n}\}
;

\bullet

if
\sigma,\tau\in\mathrm{Bal}
,
\sigma\land\tau\in\mathrm{Bal}
;

\bullet

if
\mathfrak{\sigma}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{\sigma}_{n}\in\mathrm{Bal}
and
\prod_{i=1}^{k}q_{i}\leq\prod_{j=1}^{n}\lceil\sigma_{j}\rceil
, then

\mathbf{C}^{q_{1}}\dots\mathbf{C}^{q_{n}}(\sigma_{1}\Rightarrow\dots\Rightarrow\sigma_{n}\Rightarrow o)\in\mathrm{Bal}
.

For instance, the type
\mathbf{C}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{C}^{\frac{1}{2}}o\Rightarrow o)
is balanced, while

\mathbf{C}^{1}(\mathbf{C}^{\frac{1}{2}}o\Rightarrow o)
is not.

One can check that any balanced type is also non-trivial.

To study normalization for balanced types we need a more refined notion of neutral term.

Definition E.33 (neutral terms, version 2).

For any
q\in(0,1]
, and measurable set
S
, the set
\mathrm{Neut}^{q}(S)
is defined by induction as follows:

\bullet

for any variable
x
,
x\in\mathrm{Neut}^{q}(S)
;

\bullet

if
t\in\mathrm{Neut}^{q}(S)
and for all
\omega\in S
,
\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}(\pi^{\omega}_{X}(u))\geq s
, then
tu\in\mathrm{Neut}^{qs}(S)
.

It is easily checked that for all
t\in\mathrm{Neut}^{q}(S)
and
\omega\in S
,
\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}(\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t))\geq q
.

Lemma E.34.

For any balanced and
\{[],\mathsf{hn}\}
-free type
\sigma
:

(1)

if

t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
and
\omega\in S
, then
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(S)\in\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{\lceil\sigma\rceil\cdot r}
;

(2)

\mathrm{Neut}^{q}(S)\subseteq\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,q}(S)
.

Proof.

We argue by induction on
\mathfrak{s}
:

\bullet

if
\sigma=\mathbf{C}^{q_{1}}\dots\mathbf{C}^{q_{k}}\tau
, with
\tau\in\{o,\mathsf{n}\}
, then by definition
t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
iff for all
\omega\in S
,
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\in\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{q_{1}\dots q_{k}r}=\mathrm{NF}^{\lceil o\rceil\cdot r}
. Moreover if
t\in\mathrm{Neut}^{q}(S)
, then
for all
\omega\in S
,
\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}(\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t))\geq q
, hence
t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{o}^{X,q}(S)\subseteq\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,{q}{\lceil\sigma\rceil}}(S)
.

\bullet

if
\sigma=\tau\land\rho
, then we can argue as in the corresponding point in the proof of Lemma
E.31
.

\bullet

if
\sigma=\mathbf{C}^{q_{1}}\dots\mathbf{C}^{q_{k}}(\sigma_{1}\Rightarrow\dots\Rightarrow\sigma_{n}\Rightarrow o)
, where
\prod_{i}q_{i}\leq\prod_{j}\lceil\sigma_{j}\rceil
, and
t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
, then by IH
x_{j}\in\mathrm{Neut}^{1}(S)\subseteq\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma_{i}}^{X,1}(S)
, and thus

tx_{1}\dots x_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{o}^{rq_{1}\dots q_{k}}(S)
, so for all
\omega\in S
,

\pi^{\omega}_{X}(tx_{1}\dots x_{n})=\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)x_{1}\dots x_{n}\in\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{rq_{1}\dots q_{k}}\subseteq\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{rq_{1}\dots q_{n}\prod_{j}\lceil\sigma_{j}\rceil}=\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{\lceil\sigma\rceil r}
.
We deduce then
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\in\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{\lceil\sigma\rceil r}
by applying Lemma
E.19
a finite number of times.

Moreover,
if
t\in\mathrm{Neut}^{q}(S)
,
S^{\prime}\subseteq S
,
\omega\in S^{\prime}
, and
u_{j}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma_{j}}^{X,1}(S^{\prime})
, then
by the IH for all
\omega\in S^{\prime}

\pi^{\omega}_{X}(u_{j})\in\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}^{\lceil\sigma_{j}\rceil}
, so

tu_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{Neut}^{q\prod_{k}\lceil\sigma_{j}\rceil}(S^{\prime})\subseteq\mathrm{Neut}^{qq_{1}\dots q_{k}}(S^{\prime})
;
by the IH it follows then
tu_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{o}^{X,q_{1}\dots q_{k}s}(S^{\prime})
. We can thus conclude

t\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma_{1}\Rightarrow\dots\Rightarrow\sigma_{n}\Rightarrow o}^{X,qq_{1}\dots q_{k}}(S)=\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,q}(S)
.

∎

The last ingredient of our reducibility argument is the following:

Proposition E.35.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
is derivable in
\mathbf{C}\lambda_{\to,\cap}
, where
\Gamma=\{x_{1}:\mathfrak{M}_{1},\dots,x_{m}:\mathfrak{M}_{n}\}
, then for all
S\subseteq\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rrbracket_{X}
, the following two claims hold:

(i.)

for all
u_{i}\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\mathfrak{M}_{i}}^{X,1}(S)
,
t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{m}/x_{m}]\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\mathfrak{s}}^{X,1}(S)
;

(ii.)

for all
u_{i}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{M}_{i}}^{X,1}(S)
,
t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{m}/x_{m}]\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{s}}^{X,1}(S)
.

Proof.

We only prove claim (ii.).
The argument is by induction on a type derivation of
t
:

\bullet

if
t=x
and the last rule is

\mathfrak{s}_{i}\preceq\mathfrak{t}

\mathsf{FN}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b})\subseteq X

(id
∩
)

\Gamma,x:[\mathfrak{s}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{s}_{n}]\vdash^{X}x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{t}

Then for all
S\subseteq\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rrbracket_{X}
, and for all

u_{1}\in\mathrm{NRed}^{X,1}_{\mathfrak{M}_{1}}(S),\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}^{X,1}_{\mathfrak{M}_{n}}(S)
, and

u\in\mathrm{NRed}^{X,1}_{\mathfrak{M}}(S)
,
using Lemma
E.28
we deduce
u\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{s}_{i}}^{X,1}(S)\subseteq\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{t}}^{X,1}(S)
, whence

t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n},u/x]=u\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{t}}^{X,1}(\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rrbracket_{X}\cap S)=\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{s}}^{X,1}(S)
.

\bullet

if the last rule is

\Big\{\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{j}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}\Big\}_{j=1,\dots,k}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}\bigvee_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{j}

(\vee)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

then by IH, for all
j=1,\dots,k
,
S\subseteq\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{j}\rrbracket_{X}
, for all
u_{1}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{M}_{1}}^{X,1}(S),\dots,u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{M}_{n}}^{X,1}(S)
and for all
\omega\in S
,

\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}])\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\rho}^{\emptyset,1}
.

If
k=0
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vdash\bigvee_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{j}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bot}
, so the claim trivially holds. Let then
k>0
.
Let
S\subseteq\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rrbracket_{X}\subseteq\llbracket\bigvee_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{j}\rrbracket_{X}=\bigcup_{i}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{j}\rrbracket_{X}
and
\omega\in S
. Then
f\in\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{j}\rrbracket_{X}
, for some
j\leq k
. Then first observe that from
u_{i}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{M}_{i}}^{X,q_{i}}(S)
it follows in particular that

u_{i}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{M}_{i}}^{X,1}(S\cap\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{j}\rrbracket_{X})
: for all
\omega\in S\cap\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{j}\rrbracket_{X}
, since
\omega\in S
,
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(u_{i})\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{M}_{i}}^{\emptyset,1}
, so by Lemma
E.22
we conclude that
u_{i}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{M}_{i}}^{X,1}(S\cap\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}_{j}\rrbracket_{X})
.
Hence we deduce that

\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}])\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{s}}^{\emptyset,q_{1}\dots q_{n}}
, and again by Lemma
E.22
we conclude
t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{s}}^{X,1}(S)
.

\bullet

if
t=t_{1}\oplus^{i}_{a}t_{2}
, and the last rule is

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t_{1}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\vDash(\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b})

({\oplus}l)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t_{1}\oplus^{i}_{a}t_{2}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

By IH and Lemma
E.22
, for all

S\subseteq\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rrbracket_{X}
,
\omega\in S
,

u_{1}\in\mathrm{NRed}^{X,1}_{\mathfrak{M}_{1}}(S),\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}^{X,1}_{\mathfrak{M}_{n}}(S)
,

\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t_{1}[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}])\in\mathrm{NRed}^{\emptyset,1}_{\mathfrak{s}}
.
Let now
S\subseteq\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rrbracket_{X}\subseteq\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\rrbracket_{X}\land\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rrbracket_{X}
and
u_{1}\in\mathrm{NRed}^{X,1}_{\mathfrak{M}_{1}}(S),

\dots,

u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}^{X,1}_{\mathfrak{M}_{n}}(S)
.
If now
\omega\in S\subseteq\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}}
, then
\omega(a)(i)=0
so in particular
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}])=\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t_{1}[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}])\in\mathrm{NRed}^{\emptyset,1}_{\mathfrak{s}}
.
Hence, using Lemma
E.22
we conclude that
t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]

\in

\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{s}}^{X,1}(S)
.

\bullet

if
t=t_{1}\oplus^{i}_{a}t_{2}
, and the last rule is

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t_{2}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\vDash(\lnot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}_{a}^{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b})

(\oplus r)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t_{1}\oplus^{i}_{a}t_{2}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}

then we can argue similarly to the previous case.

\bullet

if the last rule is

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma

(
\mathsf{hn}
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\mathsf{hn}

then by IH for all
S\subseteq\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rrbracket_{X}
, for all
u_{1}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{M}_{1}}^{X,1}(S),\dots,u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{M}_{n}}^{X,1}(S)
,
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,t_{n}/x_{n}])\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{\emptyset,q}
, so by Lemma

E.31
,
\mathrm{HNV}(\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,t_{n}/x_{n}]))\geq q\cdot\lceil\sigma\rceil=q
, which implies

t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,t_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathsf{hn}}^{X,q}(S)
.

\bullet

if the last rule is

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma

\sigma
safe

(
\mathsf{n}
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\mathsf{n}

then
\sigma
is
\{[],\mathsf{hn}\}
-free and balanced; by IH for all
S\subseteq\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rrbracket_{X}
, for all
u_{1}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{M}_{1}}^{X,1}(S),\dots,u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{M}_{n}}^{X,1}(S)
,
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,t_{n}/x_{n}])\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{\emptyset,q}
; furthermore, by Lemma

E.34
,
\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}(\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,t_{n}/x_{n}]))\geq q\cdot\lceil\sigma\rceil=q
, which implies

t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,t_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathsf{n}}^{X,q}(S)
.

\bullet

if
t=\lambda x.u
and the last rule is

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{M}\vdash^{X}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{s}}\tau

({\lambda})

\Gamma\vdash^{X}\lambda x.u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{s}}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\tau)

where
\vec{s}=s_{1},\dots,s_{l}
; then, by IH, for all
S\subseteq\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rrbracket_{X}
, for all

u_{1}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{M}_{1}}^{X,1}(S),\dots,u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{M}_{n}}^{X,1}(S)
,
and
v\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{X,1}(S)
,

u[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n},v/x]\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathbf{C}^{\vec{s}}\tau}^{X,1}(S)=\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,s_{1}\dots s_{k}}(S)
.
In particular, for all choice of
u_{1},\dots,u_{n}
and variable
y
not occurring free in
u_{1},\dots,u_{n}
, by letting
t^{\prime}=u[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n},y/x]
, we have that for all

v\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{X,1}(S\cap\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rrbracket_{X})
,

t^{\prime}[v/y]=u[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n},v/x]\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\tau}^{X,s_{1}\dots s_{k}}(S)
.
By Lemma
E.27
, then, we can conclude that
\lambda y.t^{\prime}=(\lambda y.t[y/x])[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]=(\lambda x.t)[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma}^{X,s_{1}\dots s_{k}}(S)=\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathbf{C}^{\vec{s}}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma)}^{X,1}(S)
.

\bullet

t=uv
and the last rule is

\Gamma\vdash^{X}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{s}}(\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma)

\Big\{\Gamma\vdash^{X}v:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}_{i}\Big\}_{i}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash^{X}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\land\left(\bigwedge_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i}\right)

({@})

\Gamma\vdash^{X}uv:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{s}}\sigma

where
\vec{s}=s_{1},\dots,s_{k}
and
{M}=[\mathfrak{s}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{s}_{n}]
, then
by IH for all
S\subseteq\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rrbracket_{X}\subseteq\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rrbracket_{X}\cap\bigcap_{i}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i}\rrbracket_{X}
and

u_{1}\in\mathrm{NRed}^{X,1}_{\mathfrak{M}_{1}}(S),\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}^{X,1}_{\mathfrak{M}_{n}}(S)
,

u[u_{1}/x,\dots,u_{n}/x]\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{M}\Rightarrow\sigma}^{X,s_{1}\dots s_{k}}(S)
and

v[u_{1}/x,\dots,u_{n}/x]\in\bigcap_{i}\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{s}_{i}}^{X,1}(S)=\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{X,1}(S)

(where, if
n=0
,
v[u_{1}/x,\dots,u_{n}/x]\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{X,1}(S)
does not follow from the induction hypothesis but from the fact that
\mathrm{NRed}_{[]}^{X,1}(S)=\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu}^{X}
) so we deduce

u[u_{1}/x,\dots,u_{n}/x]v[u_{1}/x,\dots,u_{n}/x]\ =(uv)[u_{1}/x,\dots,u_{n}/x]\ \in\mathrm{NRed}_{\sigma}^{X,s_{1}\dots s_{k}}(S)=\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathbf{C}^{\vec{s}}\sigma}^{X,1}(S)
.

\bullet

If
t=\nu a.u
and the last rule is

\Big\{\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{j}}\sigma\Big\}_{j=1,\dots,n+1}

\vDash\mu({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{j}})\geq r_{j}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}

(\mu)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}\nu a.u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{j}r_{j}q_{j}}\sigma

where
a\notin FV(\Gamma)\cup FV({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c})
, then let
S\subseteq\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rrbracket_{X}\subseteq\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rrbracket_{X}
and
u_{1}\in\mathrm{NRed}^{X,1}_{\mathfrak{M}_{1}}(S),\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{NRed}^{X,1}_{\mathfrak{M}_{n}}(S)
.
Let
T=\{\omega+\omega^{\prime}\mid\omega\in S\}\subseteq(2^{\mathbb{N}})^{X\cup\{a\}}
, which is measurable since counter-image of a measurable set through the projection function; then since
\mathsf{FN}(u_{i})\subseteq X
, we deduce using Lemma
E.22
that
u_{i}\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{M}_{i}}^{X\cup\{a\},1}(T\cap\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{j}\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})
.
By IH and the hypothesis we deduce then that

\bullet

the sets
\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{j}\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}}
are pairwise disjoint;

\bullet

u[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{NRed}^{X\cup\{a\},1}_{\mathfrak{s}}(T\cap\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{j}\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})
;

\bullet

for all
\omega\in S
,
\mu(\Pi^{\omega}(T\cap\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{j}\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}}))\geq r_{j}
;

Hence, by Lemma
E.25
we conclude that

\nu a.u[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]=(\nu a.u)[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathfrak{s}}^{X,\sum_{j}r_{j}q_{j}}(S)=\mathrm{NRed}_{\mathbf{C}^{\sum_{j}r_{j}q_{j}}\mathfrak{s}}^{X,1}(S)
.

∎

From Proposition
E.35
(i.) and Lemma
E.31
, by observing that any type of
\mathbf{C}\lambda_{\to,\cap}
is non-trivial, we deduce
the following, which generalizes the first part of Theorem
6.1
.

Theorem E.36.

If
\Gamma\vdash t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma
holds in
\mathbf{C}\lambda_{\to,\cap}
, then for all
\omega\in\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rrbracket
,
\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}(\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t))\geq q
.

From Proposition
E.35
(i.) and Lemma
E.34
we similarly deduce a generalization of Theorem
6.3
:

Theorem E.37.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma
holds in
\mathbf{C}\lambda_{\to,\cap}
, where
\Gamma
and
\mathfrak{s}
are formed of balanced types, then for all
\omega\in\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rrbracket
,
\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}(\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t))\geq q
.

From the two results above we can finally deduce the soundness part of Theorem
6.6
:

Corollary E.38.

For any closed term
t
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}(t)

\displaystyle\geq\sup\{q\mid\ \vdash t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\mathsf{hn}\}

\displaystyle\mathrm{NF}_{\rightarrowtriangle}(t)

\displaystyle\geq\sup\{q\mid\ \vdash t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\top}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}\mathsf{n}\}

E.3.
Probabilistic Reducibility Candidates for
\Lambda^{\{\}}_{\mathsf{{PE}}}
.

We now show how to adapt the family of reducibility candidates
\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{X,1}(S)
to
\Lambda^{\{\}}_{\mathsf{{PE}}}
, in order to prove the second part of Theorem
6.1
.
We will still work with the general family of types from the previous section (hence suggesting that intersection types could be added to
\mathbf{C}\lambda^{\{\}}_{\to}
, too).

For any
t\in{T}
, let
{D}^{1}_{t}:{T}\to[0,1]
be
\delta_{t}
if
t\in\mathrm{HNV}
, and if
t=\nu a.t^{\prime}
, let

{D}^{1}_{t}(u)=\mu\{\omega\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}\mid\pi^{\omega}_{\{a\}}(t^{\prime})=u\}

By recalling the definition of head-reduction in
\Lambda^{\{\}}_{\mathsf{{PE}}}
from Subsection
B.2
, one can easily check the following useful properties:

Lemma E.39.

(i.)

If
t\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}h}^{*}u
, then
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}h}^{*}\pi^{\omega}_{X}(u)
.

(ii.)

if
t\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}h}^{*}u
, then
\mathrm{HNV}(t)\geq\mathrm{HNV}(u)
.

Lemma E.40.

For all
t,u,w\in{T}
,

{D}^{1}_{t}(u)={D}^{1}_{tw}(uw)
.

Proof.

If
t
is a HNV,
tw
is a pseudo-value, so
{D}^{1}_{t}=\delta_{t}
and
{D}^{1}_{tw}=\delta_{tw}
,
which implies
{D}^{1}_{t}(u)=1
iff
t=u
iff
tw=uw
iff
{D}^{1}_{tw}=tw
.
If
t=\nu a.t^{\prime}
, since we can suppose
a\notin\mathsf{FN}(w)
, from

\pi^{\omega}_{\{a\}}(t^{\prime}w)=\pi^{\omega}_{\{a\}}(t^{\prime})w
we deduce

\{\omega\mid\pi^{\omega}_{\{a\}}(t^{\prime}w)=uw\}=\{\omega\mid\pi^{\omega}_{\{a\}}(t^{\prime})=u\}
, from which
the claim follows.
∎

The following is a simple consequence of Lemma
E.39
:

Lemma E.41.

Let
W
be a set of terms such that
t\in W
and
u\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}h}^{*}t
implies
u\in W
; then for any
t,u\in{T}
,] if
t\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}h}^{*}u
, then
\sum_{w\in W}{D}^{1}_{t}(w)\geq\sum_{w\in W}{D}^{1}_{u}(w)
.

To adapt reducibility predicates to
\Lambda^{\{\}}_{\mathsf{{PE}}}
, we need to adapt the definition of reducibility for counting quantified types.
We define yet a new family of reducibility predicates

\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{o}^{X,r}(S)\subseteq\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu,\{\}}^{X}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{[]}^{X,r}(S)

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu,\{\}}^{X}

\displaystyle\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{o}^{X,r}(S)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\mathsf{hn}}^{X,r}(S)=\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\mathsf{n}}^{X,r}(S)=\{t\mid\forall\omega\in S\ \mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}(\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t))\geq r\}

\displaystyle\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma\Rightarrow\tau}^{X,r}(S)

\displaystyle=\{t\mid\forall S^{\prime}\subseteq S,\ \forall u\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}^{X,1}(S^{\prime}),\ tu\in\mathrm{HRed}_{\tau}^{X,r}(S^{\prime})\}

\displaystyle\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma\land\tau}^{X,r}(S)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)\cap\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\tau}^{X,r}(S)

\displaystyle\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\mathbf{C}^{q}\sigma}^{X,r}(S)

\displaystyle=\left\{t\mid\forall\omega\in S\ \ \sum_{u\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\mathbf{\sigma}}^{\emptyset,r}}{D}^{1}_{\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)}(u)\geq q\right\}

Most properties of
\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}
are proved as in the corresponding cases of
\mathrm{HRed}_{\sigma}
. We here only consider the cases in which the proofs are different.

Lemma E.42.

If
t\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
and
u\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}h}^{*}t
, then
u\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
.

Proof.

We argue by induction on
\sigma
:

\bullet

if
\sigma\in\{o,\mathsf{hn},\mathsf{n}\}
, then
t\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
iff for all
\omega\in S
,
\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}(\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t))\geq r
;
the claim then follows from Lemma
E.39
(i.).

\bullet

if
\sigma=\tau\Rightarrow\rho
and
t\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
, then for all
S^{\prime}\subseteq S
and
v\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\tau}^{X,1}(S^{\prime})
,

tv\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S^{\prime})
; since
uv\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}h}^{*}tv
, by IH we deduce then
uv\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S^{\prime})
; we can thus conclude
v\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
;

\bullet

if
\sigma=\tau\land\rho
, and
t\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
, then by IH

u\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\tau}^{X,r}(S)
and
u\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S)
, so
u\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
.

\bullet

if
\sigma=\mathbf{C}^{q}\tau
and
t\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
, then

\sum_{v\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\tau}^{X,r}(S)}{D}^{1}_{\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)}(v)\geq q
, and we can conclude

\sum_{v\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\tau}^{X,r}(S)}{D}^{1}_{\pi^{\omega}_{X}(u)}(v)\geq q
from the IH and Lemma
E.41
(i).

∎

Using Lemma
E.24
(i.), whose proof is adapted without difficulties to
\Lambda^{\{\}}_{\mathsf{{PE}}}
,
Lemma
E.25
is adapted as follows:

Lemma E.43.

For all types
\sigma
, terms
t,u_{1},\dots,u_{n}
with
\mathsf{FN}(t)\subseteq X\cup\{a\}
,
\mathsf{FN}(u_{i})\subseteq X
,
and measurable set
S\subseteq(2^{\mathbb{N}})^{X\cup\{a\}}
and
S^{\prime}\subseteq(2^{\mathbb{N}})^{X}
, if

(1)

tu_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}^{X\cup\{a\},r}(S)
;

(2)

for all
\omega\in S^{\prime}
,
\mu(\Pi^{\omega}(S))\geq s
;

then
(\nu a.t)u_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma}^{X,r}(S^{\prime})
.

The following adaptation of Lemma
E.26
immediately follows from Lemma
E.42
:

Lemma E.44.

t[u/x]u_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)

\Rightarrow

(\lambda x.t)uu_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
.

Lemma E.45.

For all types
\sigma
and
\tau
,
\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\mathbf{C}^{q_{1}}\dots\mathbf{C}^{q_{k}}(\sigma\Rightarrow\tau)}^{X,r}(S)\subseteq\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma\Rightarrow\mathbf{C}^{q_{1}}\dots\mathbf{C}^{q_{k}}\tau}^{X,r}(S)
.

Proof.

For any
j\leq k
, let
U_{j}:=\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\mathbf{C}^{q_{k-j+1}}\dots\mathbf{C}^{q_{k}}(\sigma\Rightarrow\tau)}^{X,r}(S)

and

V_{j}:=\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma\Rightarrow\mathbf{C}^{q_{k-j+1}}\dots\mathbf{C}^{q_{k}}\tau}^{X,r}(S)
.

We argue, by induction on
j\leq k
, that
U_{j}\subseteq V_{j}
. If
j=0
then
U_{j}=\sigma\Rightarrow\tau=V_{j}
.

Let then
j>0
and
\omega\in S
.
Suppose

t\in U_{j}
and let

w\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}^{X,1}(S)
.

By IH we have
U_{j-1}\subseteq V_{j-1}
, from which we deduce

q_{1}\leq\sum_{u\in U_{j-1}}{D}^{1}_{\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)}(u)\ \leq\sum_{u\in V_{j-1}}{D}^{1}_{\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)}(u)

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{[Lemma \ref{lemma:dapp}]}}}{{=}}\sum_{u\in V_{j-1}}{D}^{1}_{\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\pi^{\omega}_{X}(w)}(u\pi^{\omega}_{X}(w))=\sum_{u\in V_{j-1}}{D}^{1}_{\pi^{\omega}_{X}(tw)}(u\pi^{\omega}_{X}(w))
which implies
t\in V_{j}
.

∎

We call a term
t

\nu
-safe
if
t
never reduces to a term of the form
\nu a.t^{\prime}
.

Definition E.46 (neutral terms for
\Lambda^{\{\}}_{\mathsf{{PE}}}
.).

For any
measurable set
S\subseteq(2^{\mathbb{N}})^{X}
, the set
\mathrm{Neut}_{\{\}}(S)
is defined by induction as follows:

\bullet

for any variable
x
,
x\in\mathrm{Neut}_{\{\}}(S)
;

\bullet

if
t\in\mathrm{Neut}_{\{\}}(S)
and for all
\omega\in S

\pi^{\omega}_{X}(u)
is
\nu
-safe and
\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}(u)=1
,
\{t\}u\in\mathrm{Neut}_{\{\}}(S)
.

\bullet

if
t\in\mathrm{Neut}_{\{\}}(S)
, then for all
u\in\Lambda_{\oplus,\nu,\{\}}^{X}
,
tu\in\mathrm{Neut}_{\{\}}(S)
.

It is easily checked that for all
t\in\mathrm{Neut}_{\{\}}(S)
and
\omega\in S
,
\mathrm{HNV}(\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t))=1
.

Lemma E.47.

For any type
\sigma
,

(i.)

if
\sigma
is non-trivial, then
t\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
and
\omega\in S
, then
\mathrm{HNV}(\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t))\geq\lceil\sigma\rceil\cdot r
;

(ii.)

\mathrm{Neut}_{\{\}}(S)\subseteq\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}^{X,1}(S)
.

Proof.

By induction on
\sigma
:

\bullet

if
\sigma=[]
, both claims are immediate.

\bullet

if
\sigma\in\{o,\mathsf{hn},\mathsf{n}\}
, claim (i.) holds by definition, and claim (ii.) follows from the fact that for all
t\in\mathrm{Neut}_{\{\}}(S)
and for all
\omega\in S
,
\mathrm{HNV}(\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t))=1
, so
t\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}^{X,1}(S)
.

\bullet

if
\sigma=\tau\Rightarrow\rho
and
t\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
, then, since by IH
x\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\tau}^{X,1}(S)
,
tx\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\tau}^{X,1}(S)
, hence for all
\omega\in S
,

\mathrm{HNV}(\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t))\geq\mathrm{HNV}(\pi^{\omega}_{X}(tx))\geq\lceil\rho\rceil\cdot r=\lceil\sigma\rceil\cdot r
.

Moreover, if
t\in\mathrm{Neut}_{\{\}}(S)
, then for all
S^{\prime}\subseteq S
,
u\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\tau}^{X,1}(S^{\prime})
and
\omega\in S^{\prime}
,

tu\in\mathrm{Neut}_{\{\}}(S)
, so by IH
tu\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S^{\prime})
, and we conclude then
t\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
.

\bullet

if
\sigma=\tau\land\rho
and
t\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}^{X,r}(S)
, then

t\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\tau}^{X,r}(S)
and
t\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S)
, so by IH for all
\omega\in S
,

\mathrm{HNV}(\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t))\geq\max\{r\cdot\lceil\tau\rceil,r\cdot\lceil\rho\rceil\}=r\cdot\max\{\lceil\tau\rceil,\lceil\rho\rceil\}=r\cdot\lceil\sigma\rceil
.

Moreover, it
t\in\mathrm{Neut}_{\{\}}(S)
, by IH
t\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\tau}^{X,1}(S)
and

t\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\rho}^{X,1}(S)
, whence
t\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}^{X,1}(S)
.

\bullet

if
\sigma=\mathbf{C}^{q}\tau
, and
t\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\mathbf{C}^{q}\tau}^{X,r}(S)
, let
\omega\in S
; then we consider two cases:

\bullet

if
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)
(that we can suppose being in
{T}
) is a pseudo-value, then

\sum_{w\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\tau}^{X,r}}{D}^{1}_{t}(w)\geq q>0
implies

\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\tau}^{X,r}
, so by IH we deduce

\mathrm{HNV}(\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t))\geq r\cdot\lceil\tau\rceil\geq r\cdot q\cdot\lceil\tau\rceil=r\cdot\lceil\sigma\rceil
.

\bullet

if
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)=\nu a.t^{\prime}
, then

\sum_{w\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\tau}^{X,r}}{D}^{1}_{t}(w)\geq q>0
implies, by IH,
that
\mu(\{\omega^{\prime}\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}\mid\pi^{\omega^{\prime}}(t^{\prime})\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\tau}^{\emptyset,r}\})\geq\mu(\{\omega^{\prime}\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}\mid\mathrm{HNV}(\pi^{\omega^{\prime}}(t))\geq{\lceil\tau\rceil r}\})\geq q
, and this implies

\mathrm{HNV}(\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t))\geq q\cdot r\cdot\lceil\tau\rceil=r\cdot\lceil\sigma\rceil
.

Moreover, if
t\in\mathrm{Neut}_{\{\}}(S)
, then for all
\omega\in S
,
{D}^{1}_{\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)}=\delta_{\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)}
, and since by the IH

t\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\tau}^{X,r}(S)
,
we deduce

\sum_{u\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\tau}^{X,r}}{D}^{1}_{\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)}(u)=\delta_{\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t)}(\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t))=1\geq q
,
so
t\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\mathbf{C}^{q}\tau}^{X,r}(S)
.

∎

Lemma E.48.

For any non-trivial type
\sigma
, and term
t
name-closed and
\nu
-safe, if
t\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}^{\emptyset,r}
, then

\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}(t)=1
.

Proof.

By induction on
\sigma
:

\bullet

if
\sigma\in\{o,\mathsf{hn},\mathsf{n}\}
, from
t\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}^{\emptyset,r}
we deduce
\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}(t)\geq r>0
, and since for all
u
such that
t\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}h}^{*}u
,

{D}_{u}=\delta_{u}
,
we deduce that
\sup\{\mathrm{HNV}(u)\mid t\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}h}^{*}u\}=\sup\{\sum_{v\in\mathrm{HNV}^{\{\}}}\delta_{u}(v)\mid t\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}h}^{*}u\}>0
,
this implies that
t
reduces to a HNV, whence
\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}(t)=1
.

\bullet

if
\sigma=\tau\Rightarrow\rho
and
t\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}^{\emptyset,r}
then since by Lemma
E.47

x\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\tau}^{\emptyset,1}
,

tx\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\rho}^{\emptyset,r}
;
suppose
tx\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}h}^{*}\nu a.t^{\prime}
; then since
t
is
\nu
-safe, the only possibility is that

t\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}h}^{*}(\lambda x.u)
and
u\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}h}^{*}\nu a.t^{\prime}
; but then
t\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}h}^{*}\lambda x.\nu a.t^{\prime}\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}h}\nu a.\lambda x.t^{\prime}
, against the hypothesis.
We conclude that also
tx
must be
\nu
-safe, so by IH

\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}(tx)=1
, whence
\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}(t)=1
.

\bullet

if
\sigma=\tau\land\rho
and
t\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}^{\emptyset,r}
, then
t\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\tau}^{\emptyset,r}
, so by IH,

\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}(t)=1
.

\bullet

if
\sigma=\mathbf{C}^{s}\tau
and
t\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}^{\emptyset,r}
, then
since
{D}_{t}=\delta_{t}
, from

\sum_{w\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\tau}^{\emptyset,r}}\delta_{t}(w)\geq s>0
, it follows that
t\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\tau}^{\emptyset,r}
, so by IH

\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}(t)=1

∎

Lemma E.49.

If
t\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma\Rightarrow\tau}^{X,q}(S)
and
u\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma}^{X,1}(S)
, where
\sigma
is non-trivial, then
\{t\}u\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\mathbf{C}^{s}\tau}^{X,q}(S)
.

Proof.

Let
\omega\in S
. We distinguish two cases:

\bullet

\pi^{\omega}_{X}(u)\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}h}^{*}\nu a.u^{\prime}
;
then, letting

W_{\sigma}:=\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}^{X,1}(S)
and

W_{\tau}:=\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\tau}^{X,q}(S)
, we have that

\sum_{w\in W}{D}_{\pi^{\omega}_{X}(\nu a.tu^{\prime})}^{1}(w)=\sum_{w\in W}\mu(\{\omega^{\prime}\mid\pi^{\omega+\omega^{\prime}}_{X\cup\{a\}}(tu^{\prime})=w\}=\mu\{\omega^{\prime}\mid\pi^{\omega+\omega^{\prime}}_{X\cup\{a\}}(tu^{\prime})\in W\}=\mu\{\omega^{\prime}\mid t\pi^{\omega+\omega^{\prime}}_{X\cup\{a\}}(u^{\prime})\in W\}\geq\mu\{\omega^{\prime}\mid\pi^{\omega+\omega^{\prime}}_{X\cup\{a\}}(u^{\prime})\in U\}=\sum_{w\in U}{D}^{1}_{\nu a.u}(w)\geq s
, which proves that
\nu a.tu^{\prime}\in W_{\tau}
. Since
\{t\}u\rightarrowtriangle_{\{\}h}^{*}\nu a.tu^{\prime}
, we conclude then
\{t\}u\in W_{\tau}

from Lemma
E.42
.

\bullet

\pi^{\omega}_{X}(u)
is
\nu
-safe; then from
\pi^{\omega}_{X}(u)\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\sigma}^{\emptyset,q}
we deduce, by Lemma
E.48
, that
\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}(\pi^{\omega}_{X}(u))=1
. We deduce then that
\{t\}u\in\mathrm{Neut}_{\{\}}(S)
, and by Lemma
E.47
we conclude
\{t\}u\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma}^{X,q}(S)
.

∎

Proposition E.50.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
is derivable in
\mathbf{C}\lambda^{\{\}}_{\to}
, where
\Gamma=\{x_{1}:\mathfrak{s}_{1},\dots,x_{m}:\mathfrak{s}_{n}\}
, then for all
S\subseteq\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rrbracket_{X}
, for all
u_{i}\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\mathfrak{s}_{i}}^{X,1}(S)
,
t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{m}/x_{m}]\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\mathfrak{s}}^{X,1}(S)
.

Proof.

We can argue as in the proof of Proposition
E.35
, with the following two new cases:

\bullet

if
t=\{t_{1}\}t_{2}
and the last rule is

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t_{1}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}(\mathfrak{s}\Rightarrow\sigma)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t_{2}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{s}\mathfrak{s}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}

(
\{\}
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{s}\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}\sigma

then by IH for all
u_{i}\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\mathfrak{s}_{i}}^{X,1}(S)
,
t_{1}[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}(\mathfrak{s}\Rightarrow\sigma)}^{X,1}(S)
and

t_{2}[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\mathbf{C}^{s}\mathfrak{s}}^{X,1}(S)
;
then by Lemma
E.45
and Lemma
E.49
(observing that
\mathfrak{s}
is non-trivial) we deduce

(\{t_{1}\}t_{2})[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]=\{t_{1}[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\}(t_{2}[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}])\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\mathbf{C}^{\vec{q}}\sigma}^{X,1}(S)
.

\bullet

If
t=\nu a.u
and the last rule is

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\land{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q_{j}}\sigma

\vDash\mu({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}})\geq r

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\vDash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}

(\mu)

\Gamma\vdash^{X}\nu a.u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{r}\sigma

where
a\notin FV(\Gamma)\cup FV({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c})
, then let
S\subseteq\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rrbracket_{X}\subseteq\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c}\rrbracket_{X}
and
u_{1}\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}^{X,1}_{\mathfrak{s}_{1}}(S),\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}^{X,1}_{\mathfrak{s}_{n}}(S)
.
Let
T=\{g+f\mid\omega\in S\}\subseteq(2^{\mathbb{N}})^{X\cup\{a\}}
, which is measurable since counter-image of a measurable set through the projection function; then since
\mathsf{FN}(u_{i})\subseteq X
, we deduce using Lemma
E.22
that
u_{i}\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\mathfrak{s}_{i}}^{X\cup\{a\},1}(T\cap\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{j}\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})
.
By IH and the hypothesis we deduce then that

\bullet

u[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}^{X\cup\{a\},1}_{\mathfrak{s}}(T\cap\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{j}\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})
;

\bullet

for all
\omega\in S
,
\mu(\Pi^{\omega}(T\cap\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d}_{j}\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}}))\geq r
;

Hence, by Lemma
E.43
we conclude that

\nu a.u[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]=(\nu a.u)[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\mathfrak{s}}^{X,\sum_{j}r_{j}q_{j}}(S)=\mathrm{H^{*}Red}_{\mathbf{C}^{r}\mathfrak{s}}^{X,1}(S)
.

∎

We can now deduce Theorem
6.1
from Proposition
E.50
as in the case of
\mathbf{C}\lambda_{\to,\cap}
.

Theorem E.51.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{X}t:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}
, then for all
\omega\in\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}b}\rrbracket
,
\mathrm{HNV}_{\rightarrowtriangle}(\pi^{\omega}_{X}(t))\geq\lceil\sigma\rceil
.
